<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Tajika astrology, specifically focusing on the variations of the Kamboola yoga (a planetary configuration). It follows the classification of these configurations into "best," "medium," and "worst" categories based on the strength of the planets involved.</meta>

When this yoga occurs, it is called a "low" *Kamboola* <note>Kamboola is a configuration in Tajika astrology where the Moon connects two planets that are already in a specific relationship (Ithashala)</note>; through this, the attainment of a kingdom is achieved only with great difficulty. || 46 ||

Now, the author describes the characteristics of the "lowest of the low" *Kamboola*. When the Lord of the Ascendant <note>The planet ruling the rising sign</note> and the Lord of the Matter <note>The planet ruling the specific house related to the question, e.g., children or wealth</note> are both in their signs of debilitation <note>Nicha: the zodiac sign where a planet is at its weakest</note> or in the signs of their enemies, and they form a *Muthasila* <note>A specific aspect or connection between two planets</note> with each other, and the Moon—also being in its sign of debilitation or an enemy's sign—forms a *Muthasila* with them, then that *Kamboola* is called the "lowest of the low." Why? Because the Moon and the other two planets have all acquired "lowly" status.

**Example:** In a query regarding the birth of a son (*Putralabha*), if the Ascendant is Sagittarius, its lord Jupiter is in Capricorn (his debilitation). The lord of the 5th house (children) is Mars, who is in Cancer (his debilitation). The Moon is also in Scorpio (its debilitation) or Pisces. Here, if these three form a mutual *Muthasila* yoga, it is the "lowest of the low" *Kamboola*. One should declare that there will be no gain of progeny at all. || 47 ||

Thus, the sixteen variations of *Kamboola* have been stated. Now, among the previously mentioned variations, he provides examples of the "excellent" and "medium" *Kamboola* yogas:

-> When the Moon and the other planets are in their signs of debilitation or enemy signs, <-
-> that *Itthashala-Kamboola* is called the "lowest of the low." || 47 || <-
-> If the Sun or Mars is in Aries, and the Moon is in Taurus or Cancer, <-
-> then that *Itthashala-Kamboola* is "excellent" and fulfills the task. || 48 || <-
-> If the Moon is in Scorpio and Mars is in Cancer, and they form an *Itthashala*, <-
-> it is the "lowest of the low" *Kamboola*, causing the destruction of the work and sorrow. || 49 || <-

He explains the example of the "excellent" and "medium" *Kamboola* yogas mentioned in the verse "If in Aries...". In a query about children, if the Ascendant is Aries, its lord Mars is in Aries (his own home). The lord of the 5th house, the Sun, is in Aries (his sign of exaltation). There is a mutual *Muthasila* yoga between these two (the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter). If the Moon is in its own sign of Cancer or in Taurus, and the Moon forms a *Muthasila* with them, then that *Kamboola* is "excellent," meaning it fulfills the task and gives the desired fruit. || 48 ||

Now he gives the example of the final *Kamboola* yoga called the "lowest of the low" in the verse "If in Scorpio...". In a query about the gain of a son, if the Ascendant is Sagittarius, its lord Jupiter is in Capricorn (debilitated). The lord of the house of children, Mars, is in Cancer (debilitated). The Moon is in Scorpio (debilitated). If a *Muthasila* yoga is known between the Moon and Mars, and between Jupiter and Mars, the "lowest of the low" *Kamboola* is begun, which results in the destruction of the desired work.

<summary>This page details the specific planetary conditions that create "excellent" versus "lowest" Kamboola yogas in Tajika astrology, using the birth of a son as a primary case study. It emphasizes that planets in debilitated or enemy signs lead to failure, while exalted or home-sign planets lead to success.</summary>

It gives results equal to the *Sakambūla* yoga. **Example:** In a question regarding the attainment of happiness, Leo is the Ascendant; its lord, the Sun, is exalted in Aries. When these three form a mutual *Muthaśila* <note>an aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one</note>, it is called the "Low-Excellent" *Kambūla*. In this way, the attainment of happiness is achieved with some effort. || 44 || Now, he describes the characteristics of the "Low-Medium" *Kambūla*. "The Moon," etc. If the Moon is in its sign of debilitation or in the house of an enemy, but is in its own *Haddā* <note>a specific subdivision of a sign, also known as 'term'</note>, or in its own *Drekkāṇa* <note>a 10-degree subdivision</note>, or in its own <term>Navāṃśa</term> <note>the ninth part of a zodiac sign, crucial for determining a planet's true strength</note>, and forms a *Muthaśila* with the lord of the matter being questioned, it becomes an *Itthaśāla* <note>a favorable configuration</note>. This is called "Low-Medium" *Kambūla*. Why? Because the Moon has low dignity while the other two planets have medium dignity. **Example:** In a question regarding the birth of a son, Virgo—

->If the Moon is in its debilitation or an enemy's house, but is in its own *Haddā* and such,<-
->and forms an *Itthaśāla* there, it is called Low-Medium *Kambūla*. || 45 ||<-

->If the Moon is in its debilitation or an enemy's house, and forms an *Itthaśāla* with planets<-
->lacking dignity, it is known as Low *Kambūla*, giving results as previously described. || 46 ||<-

is the Ascendant; its lord, Mercury, is in Capricorn in its own *Haddā*. The lord of the House of Children, Saturn, is in Pisces in its own *Haddā* at five degrees. The Moon is in its debilitation, Scorpio, at three degrees. When these three form a mutual *Muthaśila* yoga, it becomes "Low-Medium" *Kambūla*. Through this, the attainment of progeny should be known as achievable with a small amount of effort. || 45 || Now, he describes the characteristics of another, the "Low" *Kambūla*. "The Moon," etc. If the Moon is in its own sign of debilitation or its enemy's sign, and forms a mutual *Muthaśila* with the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter who are both "quarter-less" <note>lacking the 'Excellent' or 'Medium' dignities</note>, then it is known as "Low" *Kambūla*. Why? Because the Moon has attained low dignity and the other two have equal (low) dignity. This gives results equal to the *Muṣka-Kambūla* yoga mentioned before. **Example:** In a question regarding the attainment of a kingdom <note>or high office</note>, the Lord of the Ascendant, Venus, is in the <unclear>fifteenth</unclear> sign, Leo. The Lord of the Kingdom, Saturn, is in Taurus at ten degrees. The Moon is in its debilitation, Scorpio, at three degrees. Their *Muthaśila*—

<summary>This page defines the "Low-Medium" and "Low" variations of the Kambūla yoga, explaining how the dignity (strength) of the Moon and other significators determines the ease with which a desired outcome, such as happiness or children, is achieved.</summary>

Similarly, when the Moon is in Capricorn and forms a favorable aspect <note>original: "shubhashile," referring to a positive Ithashala or Muthashila aspect</note> with Jupiter, who is also in its sign of debilitation <term>debilitation: the zodiac sign where a planet's influence is weakest; for Jupiter, this is Capricorn</term>, a "middling-low" Kambula yoga is formed. Why? Because here the Moon is in its own decan <term>decan: a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</term> and its own high dignity, while Jupiter is debilitated. In this specific case, a decan dignity for Jupiter is not possible. Likewise, when the Moon is in Pisces and forms a Muthashila aspect with Mercury, who is in its sign of debilitation (Pisces), a "middling-low" Kambula yoga is formed. Why? Because here the Moon is in its own decan and its own sign in the divisional chart <term>amsha: a divisional harmonic chart used for fine-tuning predictions</term>, while Mercury is debilitated. These five examples are considered "auspicious," meaning they still grant some positive results despite the debilitation.

|| 51 || Now, the author refutes a certain opinion using an illustration. Regarding the statement "That is incorrect": The opinion held by some—that the Muthashila and other aspects should only be considered when the fast-moving and slow-moving planets are in the same zodiac sign—is incorrect; it is not supported by logic. Why? He explains: if between the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnadhisha: the planet ruling the rising sign</term> and the Lord of the Matter <term>Karyadhisha: the planet ruling the specific life-topic being questioned</term>, an Ithashala aspect is formed by one planet being in its sign of debilitation with another planet also in its sign of debilitation, it is said that the work will not be accomplished. Indeed, two planets do not usually share the same sign of debilitation.

->That is incorrect; an Ithashala aspect between a debilitated planet and another debilitated planet, or between enemies, causes the destruction of the work; this is clearly established there. || 52 ||<-

->When there is an Ithashala between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter, if even one is debilitated and lacks dignity like its own sign, and the other is not aspected by the Moon, it creates a Kambula yoga [of a destructive type]. || 53 ||<-

Furthermore, it is stated that when the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter are mutual enemies, the Muthashila aspect between them destroys the work. For this reason, in the *Tajika Samjna Tantra* composed by Samarasingha <note>Samarasingha was a 13th-century scholar who translated Persian astrological concepts into Sanskrit</note>, the following is clearly stated:

> If a debilitated planet forms a Muthashila with another debilitated planet, or an enemy with an enemy, that Kambula is destroyed, and the Moon also becomes a destroyer in this configuration. || 1 ||

Here, the second configuration can occur even when the planets are in the same sign; therefore, it should not be presented as a flaw. The author has written this based on the context of the foundational source texts. || 52 || Now, he describes another variation of the Kambula yoga to understand the production of results in a different way. "Lord of the Ascendant and Matter...": Previously, Kambula was defined as the Moon forming a Muthashila with the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter when they are in the same sign. Now, the distinction in their planetary authority is being discussed. Where the Lord of the Ascendant...

## Chapter 2

The *Sandeh* <note>A specific technical term in Tajika astrology related to planetary strength</note> gives zero. || 49 || Now, regarding the first types of *Kambool* <note>A yoga where the Moon transfers influence between two planets</note>, the author explains that examples of these variations should be inferred. Thus: In this way, the application of examples for the previously mentioned variations should be inferred and contemplated by wise astrologers based on the relationship of the stated characteristics. Why are the examples not explicitly stated? Because the sentences defining the characteristics are already clear in their meaning. || 50 ||

Here, some authorities claim that the *Muthasila* <note>Also known as Ithasala; a yoga of "connection" where a faster planet approaches a slower one</note> yoga occurs only when the fast-moving and slow-moving planets are in the same zodiac sign. He presents their opinion: When the Moon is in Aries and forms a *Muthasila* yoga with Saturn, who is also in Aries, then this is called a "Medium-Medium" *Kambool*. Based on such examples, because the fast and slow planets are in the same sign, others do not accept *Muthasila* and similar yogas occurring across different signs. The word "others" refers to authorities like *Etasarafa* and others. Accordingly, Samarasimha <note>A famous 13th-century authority on Tajika astrology</note> states: With the Moon in Aries, the Sun in... <note>The text here lists specific planetary positions to illustrate the grades of the yoga</note>.

-> Thus, the application of examples for the previously mentioned variations || should be inferred by the wise from the connection of the stated characteristics. || 50 || <-
-> From examples such as the Moon in Aries, others have declared that *Ithasala* and other yogas || occur only when the slow and fast planets are in the same sign. || 51 || <-

...with Mercury in Pisces and the Ascendant. || 1 || The meaning is this: When the Moon is in Aries—since no other location is specified—and forms a *Muthasila* with Saturn who is also in Aries, a "Medium-Medium" *Kambool* is produced. Here, with the Moon in Aries, it has no dignity of "House" or "Exaltation"; it does not have its own *Drekkana* <note>A 10-degree subdivision of a sign</note>, but usually has its own *Navamsha* <note>A 3-degree 20-minute subdivision</note>. Saturn, however, is in its "Fall" <note>Debilitation</note> in Aries. 

Similarly, when the Moon is in Cancer and forms a mutual *Muthasila* with Mars, who is in his "Fall" in Cancer, an "Excellent-Poor" *Kambool* is produced. Why? Because here the Moon is in its own House <note>Cancer is ruled by the Moon</note>, but Mars is in his Fall. 

Likewise, when the Moon is in Virgo and a *Muthasila* is declared with Venus, who is in her "Fall" in Virgo, a "Medium-Poor" *Kambool* is produced. Why? The Moon has its own *Navamsha*, but Venus is in her Fall. Here too, the Moon does not have its own *Drekkana*...

The term "own exaltation" <note>Sanskrit: *svoccum*</note> and the phrase "beginning with" implies a planet situated in its own decan <term>Dreshkana; a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</term>, its own ninth-part <term>Navamsha; a 3.25-degree subdivision</term>, its own fall, or its own enemy’s sign. This means the planet is devoid of or separated from these specific positional strengths. || 56 || Therefore, if such a Moon does not form a *Muthashila* <term>Muthashila; an auspicious aspect or 'application' between two planets within a specific orb of influence</term> with either the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnadhisha</term> or the Lord of the Matter <term>Karyadhisha; the planet representing the specific subject of the inquiry</term>, but this Moon—while currently at the very end of its current sign—is about to enter another sign <note>original: *dharmaprima*, likely a corruption of the Persian *daharaprima* or 'boundary'</note>, and if the sign it enters is the home or exaltation of a certain planet, and if the Moon then forms a *Muthashila* with that planet residing there, then this is known as the *Gairi-Kambulu* Yoga. This *Gairi-Kambulu* should be understood to yield results similar to the previously mentioned *Kambulu* Yoga. However, there is a distinction: if the Moon, having moved to the other sign, does not receive a *Muthashila* from the lord of that sign, or forms a *Muthashila* with a planet lacking essential dignity, then that *Gairi-Kambulu* is recorded as producing inauspicious results.

---

-> If the Moon enters another sign and forms an *Itthashala* <term>Itthashala; another term for the Muthashila or 'reaching' aspect</term> with a planet in its own dignity or exaltation, the wise call this *Gairi-Kambulu*; through this, the result is said to be auspicious. || 57 || <-
-> In a query asking "Will I obtain happiness?", Leo is the Ascendant and the Sun is in Aries. Furthermore, Mars is in Aquarius at twelve degrees. || 58 || <-
-> The Moon is situated in the final degree of Virgo, forming an *Itthashala*. Being devoid of its own sign or other dignities, it does not have a *Muthashila* with either [the Sun or Mars]. || 59 || <-

|| 57 || Now, the example of this *Gairi-Kambulu* is explained in three verses. "Will I obtain...": when a query is made asking "Will I obtain happiness or not?" At that time, Leo is the Ascendant. Its lord, the Sun, is in Aries at eighteen degrees. The house of happiness is the fourth house, named Scorpio; its lord, Mars, is in Aquarius at twelve <note>original: *ravibhir*, a numerical code where 'Suns' equals 12</note> degrees. Between these two—the Lord of the Ascendant (Sun) and the Lord of the Matter (Mars)—there is a mutual *Muthashila* aspect. In this configuration, the Moon <note>Sanskrit: *Indu*</note> is at the very last degree of Virgo (the 30th degree). Here, the Moon is devoid of its own sign or other auspicious dignities and does not have a *Muthashila* with either the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Matter. However, the Moon is about to enter the house of Libra. That house of Libra is the exaltation of Saturn.

# Chapter 2

The combination of two planetary configurations is now considered. Among them, one planet is situated in its own sign or its exaltation. The other, the second planet, lacks these specific positions like its own sign <note>original: "svarkshadi-pada-hina"</note> but possesses its own planetary strength through its own exaltation, its own aspect, its own decan <term>Drekkana: a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</term>, its own ninth-part <term>Navamsha: a 3-degree 20-minute subdivision used to determine a planet's true strength</term>, or its own fall or own field. This means it has reached an "equal authority" <note>original: "samadhikara"</note>. In this context, if the Moon, having reached this equal authority, forms a *Muthashila* <term>Muthashila: an aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the fulfillment of a task</term>, then the yoga called *Kamboola* occurs. || 53 ||

Now, the author explains the result of this with an example. In that case, in this *Kamboola* yoga, one should understand there will be a "smallness of results" <note>the goal is achieved, but only partially</note>. Here is the illustration: Just as a man of a different caste <note>lower caste</note> requesting wealth from a high-born twice-born <term>Dvija: a member of the three upper castes</term> might receive only a small amount of wealth—this is the implied meaning. In the same way, the poets and scholars understand this *Kamboola*. This means that if one requests from a servant-class person, they might occasionally give a little out of embarrassment. || 54 ||

> In that case, one should know the results are meager, just as when one of another caste requests from a high-born man. The poets know this to be the nature of this yoga. || 54 ||
> He whose authority—whether through his own sign or others—is neither auspicious nor inauspicious, and whose form is not seen by anyone <note>receives no aspects from other planets</note>, is considered "one traveling a lonely path" <term>Shunyadhvaga: the Sanskrit equivalent of 'Void of Course'</term>. || 55 ||
> Between the Lord of the Action and the Lord of the Work, if the Moon is "traveling a lonely path," and because it lacks positions like exaltation, it forms no *Ithashala* <term>Ithashala: a favorable planetary aspect</term> with anyone... || 56 ||

Now, regarding the *Nair-Kamboola* yoga, the author describes the characteristics of the "lonely path traveler" <note>Shunyadhvaga</note> as it is useful for that definition. For whichever planet there is no auspicious authority—meaning its own house, exaltation, aspect, decan, or ninth-part—which would give good results; and likewise, there is no inauspicious authority—meaning it is not in its fall or a শত্রু sign—which would give bad results; and further, if it is not "seen" <note>aspected</note> by any planet, whether benefic or malefic, it is called "one traveling a lonely path." This is stated because it does not encounter any company on its journey. || 55 ||

Having established the definition of the "lonely path traveler," he explains the definition of *Raikamboola* in two verses. Regarding the aforementioned Lord of the Sign and Lord of the Work, if the Moon is "traveling a lonely path," it moves along a void way. How does it become a "void way"? He says: because it lacks positions such as exaltation...

## Now, the definition of the Radda Yoga <note>The "Cancellation" or "Rescinded" configuration</note> is stated.

Regarding the setting <note>combust</note> and so on: A planet that is set <note>too close to the Sun</note>, or is in its sign of debilitation, or is in the house of its enemy, or is retrograde, or is "lacking light"—meaning its brilliance is diminished or it is devoid of luster—such a planet is to be understood as weak. If a weak planet forms a *Muthasila* <note>an aspect of application where a faster planet approaches a slower one</note> with any bright planet, it will not be able to accomplish the desired task, whether at the end, the middle, or the beginning. This is because a planet of ill-fortune is not capable or powerful enough to withstand great brilliance.

|| 62 ||

Now, the author describes the ripening of the fruit <note>the timing of results</note> based on the specific placement and timing of such a weak planet. Regarding the placement in a *Kendra* <note>an angular house: 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note>: If the planet ruling the matter is weak and situated in a *Kendra*, and it joins with or forms a *Muthasila* with the Lord of the Ascendant situated in an *Apoklima* <note>a cadent house: 3rd, 6th, 9th, or 12th</note>, then the specific task appears to progress at first but ultimately fails at the end.

> If a planet that is set, debilitated, in an enemy's house, retrograde, or lacking light forms a *Muthasila* aspect, ||
> It is not capable of leading, because in this great orb <note>the zodiac</note>, it cannot accomplish the task at the beginning or the end. || 62 ||
> If a planet in a *Kendra* joins one in an *Apoklima*, the task is destroyed at the end after starting well. ||
> If the planet is in an *Apoklima* and joins one in a *Kendra*, it is destroyed at first but happens later. || 63 ||
> If a slow-moving planet is in its own sign and is joined by a fast planet that lacks such dignity, the work is successful. ||
> Even there, it should be said the task happens, unless the fast planet is retrograde or otherwise powerless. || 64 ||

Conversely, if a planet in an *Apoklima* house joins with one in a *Kendra* house through a *Muthasila* aspect, then it should be said that after initially failing or not being obtained, the specific task is eventually accomplished. || 63 ||

## Now, the definition of the Dushphalikutha Yoga <note>The "Bad-Fruit-Cutter" or "Fruitless" configuration</note> is stated.

Regarding the slow-moving planet: If a slow-moving planet <term>Manda-gati</term> <note>usually Saturn or Jupiter</note> is situated in its own sign <note>Sva-bho</note> or its own "varga" <note>divisional strengths like the Decan or Navamsha</note>, and an *Itthashala* <note>a favorable aspect</note> is formed by a fast-moving planet that is "without status"—meaning it lacks dignity such as being in its own sign or exaltation—the desired task will still be accomplished. However, this is only if the fast-moving planet is not rendered powerless by retrogradation or other debilities. || 64 ||

<summary>This page defines the Radda and Dushphalikutha Yogas, explaining how planetary debilities like combustion or poor house placement (Apoklima) cause a predicted outcome to fail or be delayed. It emphasizes that even a weak fast planet can succeed if the slower planet it aspects is strongly placed in its own sign.</summary>

# Chapter 2

Because Saturn is in the sign of Libra, and due to its similar speed of motion, another planet located in Libra that forms an aspect with the Moon creates a *Muthasila* <note>original: "मुथशिल" - a Tajika term derived from the Arabic 'Ittisal', meaning the application of an aspect between two planets</note> yoga. This specific planetary arrangement is known as *Gairika-Kambula* <note>original: "गैरिकंवूल" - a variation of the Kambula yoga where a third planet assists in the connection</note>. Its specific result is described as follows: through the assistance of a third planet, one attains the desired success and form of the work intended.

### Example Horoscope Chart

In this specific field of yoga, if a planet like Mercury is situated in the sign of Libra and possesses no specific dignity—neither auspicious nor inauspicious—and the Moon, moving there, forms a *Muthasila* with that Mercury, then it is known as the unfavorable *Gairika-Kambula*. On this specific example, there is a lack of consensus, as it was not mentioned by the authority Samarasimha <note>A famous 13th-century author on Tajika astrology</note>.

---

Now, the definition of the *Khallasara* yoga is stated. The term "void" <note>original: "शून्य" (shunya)</note> is used here. In this context, the phrase "by any one [planet]" should be understood as implied, as also stated by Samarasimha:

"When the Moon is without a connection to a planet of power, it is not adorned. **Gairika-Kambula** provides gain through the help of another. When the Moon is in a 'void of sound' <note>original: "शून्यध्वनि" (shunyadhvani) - likely referring to the 'Void of Course' Moon</note> between two planets, having no *Ithasala* <note>aspect</note> and no conjunction, it is *Khallasara*. This is not auspicious; it destroys the fruits of the *Kambula* yoga."

->Gairika-Kambula<-

A *Muthasila* yoga between the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnadhisha: the planet ruling the first house of the horoscope</term> and the Lord of the Matter <term>Karyadhisha: the planet ruling the specific house related to the question asked</term> is certainly required. In that context, according to the previously mentioned characteristics, the Moon is in a "void of sound" or a "void path" <note>The 'Void of Course' Moon, where the Moon makes no further major aspects before leaving its current sign</note>. Such a Moon exists when there is a *Muthasila* yoga with only one of the two—either the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Matter. Or, if such a Moon is without a conjunction with the Lord of Gain or the Lord of the Matter, that configuration is known as *Khallasara*. This is not auspicious. In what way? It is a destroyer of the *Kambula* fruit. When a *Muthasila* yoga exists, the *Kambula* yoga becomes full or complete. But here, due to the absence of a *Muthasila* yoga with anyone, the strength and results of the yoga are destroyed by the aforementioned nature.

# Now, the definition of the influential planet is stated.

In this context, the word *Kuttha* <note>original: "Kuttha"; a technical term in Tajika or Perso-Arabic astrology adopted into Sanskrit</note> is used in the language of the Greeks <note>original: "Yavana-bhasha"; referring to the Perso-Arabic or Hellenistic astrological tradition</note> to mean a "strong planet" (*Vali-graha*). The author, Vallabha, describes certain types of these planets in the first verse.

**Regarding the Ascendant:** Here, the connection is that a planet is "strong." Among all positions, a planet situated in the Ascendant (*Lagna*) <note>the first house or the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon</note> is the strongest. In its absence, a planet in a "Center" (*Kendra*)—the fourth, seventh, or tenth houses—is strong, though less powerful than one in the Ascendant. In the absence of that, a planet in a "Succedent" house (*Panaphara*)—the second, fifth, eighth, or eleventh houses—is strong, but less so than one in a Center. Failing that, a planet that "looks at the Ascendant" (*Vilagna-darshi*), situated in the third or ninth houses, is strong. Alternatively, the relationship of adjective and noun can be applied to "nearby" or "slightly viewing the Ascendant." This means a planet situated in the third, sixth, ninth, or eleventh houses, which is not in a Center but in a Succedent or Cadent (*Apoklima*) house and views the Ascendant, is considered strong.

> If in the Ascendant, or in a Center, or nearby, or viewing the Ascendant; if in its own house, its exaltation, or its own division (*Hadda*); if in its own *Musala* or *Hetve*; if it is a strong planet, moving at average speed, and not fast. || 67 ||
>
> One that has risen, one in direct motion, one joined with or aspected by benefics; one not reached or joined by the gaze of a malefic planet; and at evening, Venus, the Moon, and Mars are strong. || 68 ||

Others say that by the word "nearby," the Succedent and Cadent houses (excluding the sixth and twelfth) are included; even if they are at the end of a sign, if they are mathematically calculated to be close, they are somehow considered strong. Furthermore, a planet is strong when it is in its own house, its exaltation (*Uccha*), its decan (*Drekkana*), or in its own *Musala*, *Hetve*, or *Hadda* <note>these are specific technical subdivisions of zodiac signs used to determine planetary strength</note>. Likewise, a planet whose "clear motion" (*Spashta-gati*) is equal to the "mean motion" (*Madhya-gati*) <note>the average speed of a planet</note> is strong. Also, one that is "not fast" (*Ashighra*)—meaning its motion is slower than its average speed—is considered strong. || 67 ||

Now, other types are stated. **Regarding the rising:** A planet that has "made its rise" (*Kritodaya*)—meaning it is visible—is strong. Also, one in "direct motion" (*Margagati*) is strong. Likewise, one joined with or "eyed" (aspected) by a "benefic" (*Shubha*) <note>a naturally auspicious planet like Jupiter or Venus</note> is strong. Furthermore, one that is not reached or joined by the "gaze" (*Drishti*) of a "malefic" (*Krura*) <note>a naturally inauspicious planet like Saturn or Mars</note> is strong. And...

<summary>This page defines the 'Kuttha' or strong planet in Tajika astrology, ranking planetary power based on house placement (Centers vs. Succedents), visibility, speed of motion, and relationships with benefic or malefic planets.</summary>
<keywords>Kuttha, Ascendant (Lagna), Centers (Kendra), Succedent (Panaphara), Cadent (Apoklima), Direct Motion (Margagati), Benefics, Malefics, Tajika Astrology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary strength and specific yogas (astrological combinations) from the previous folio, specifically focusing on the 'Vira' and 'Tambuya' configurations in Tajika astrology.</meta>

The definition of a weak position is when a planet is in the setting house <note>the 7th house</note>, its sign of debilitation, an enemy's sign, or is retrograde and lacking in light; in such cases, it is considered powerless. || 64 || Now, the author describes the *Vira* <note>literally 'Heroic' or 'Assisted'</note> yoga and its variations. Regarding the lack of strength:

When the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnadhipati: the planet ruling the rising sign</term> and the Lord of the Matter <term>Karyadhipati: the planet ruling the house associated with the specific question</term> are both lacking in strength—meaning they are powerless due to being in the setting house, debilitated, in an enemy's sign, or retrograde as previously stated—if one of these two (the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Matter) forms a connection <note>original: "itthamahala" or "mushila"</note> with a third planet that is strong by being in its own sign or its own division, then through the assistance of that third party, the desired result is achieved very quickly. This is the yoga of assistance. Alternatively, if any other two planets are strong and in their own signs and one of them forms a *mushila* <note>an aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one</note> with either the Lord of the Ascendant or the Lord of the Matter, the wise say the task will be accomplished through the help of others.

->When the Lords of the Matter and the Ascendant are weak, but one is joined by another in its own sign,<-
->or when two others are strong, the wise say the work is done through the help of another. || 65 ||<-
->A strong planet at the end of a sign, moving to the next, giving its light by degrees—<-
->wherever this occurs, it is the *Tambuya* yoga, which accomplishes the task. || 66 ||<-

This completes the description of the *Vira* yoga. || 65 || Now, the author describes the characteristics of the *Tambuya* <note>a Persian-derived term in Tajika astrology referring to the 'transfer of light'</note> yoga. When there is no direct *mushila* yoga between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Matter, but one of them is a strong planet located at the very end of a sign (at the 29th degree) and is about to enter the next sign, it gives its own brilliance <term>Deeptamsha: the orb of light or influence of a planet</term> to a planet situated in that next sign. This is known as the *Tambuya* yoga. If the planet in the next sign is favorably aspected, this yoga becomes a "doer of the work," meaning it fulfills the desired objective. || 66 ||

<summary>This page defines the Vira and Tambuya yogas, explaining how a weak planetary ruler can still achieve success through the assistance of a third, stronger planet or by transferring its influence into a new zodiacal sign.</summary>
<keywords>Vira yoga, Tambuya yoga, Mushila, Lord of the Ascendant, Lord of the Matter, planetary strength, Tajika astrology, transfer of light</keywords>

--- Page 72 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary strength and debility, specifically focusing on the conditions that render a planet "powerless" or "weak" in a horary or natal chart.</meta>

Starting from the twelfth house, those planets situated in the six signs <note>the half of the zodiac starting from the 12th house backwards</note> are considered strong. This means that those residing in the middle, from the tenth house until the third, are strong. Likewise, male planets are established in their strength when situated in odd-numbered signs <note>Aries, Gemini, etc.</note>. Others, the neutral planets, are strong in even-numbered signs. Having reflected upon and deliberated over these various powerful divisions previously mentioned, one should declare the specific inauspicious results. || 70 ||

Now, the characteristics of "evil results" are described. Here, in the <term>Yavana language<note>original: "Yavana Bhasha"; refers to Greek or Perso-Arabic astrological traditions integrated into Indian practice</note></term>, this is referred to by the term *Durapha* <note>likely a Sanskritization of the Arabic/Persian 'Du'l-quwwat' or related terms for weakness</note>. This is of many types. Among them, the first verse explains several types. "From the Ascendant..."—this refers to that which is <term>powerless<note>original: "Nirvala"</note></term>. Any planet situated in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth house from the Ascendant <note>the "Lagna" or rising sign</note> becomes powerless. Likewise, one that is not moving straight but is in <term>retrograde motion<note>original: "Vakragami"</note></term>.

### If a planet is in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth house from the Ascendant, is retrograde, is joined with cruel planets, or is combust; or if it forms a "Shila" <note>a specific planetary configuration</note> with planets that are cruel, in their sign of fall, or in an enemy's sign...

### ...or if it is aspected by a "small sight" <note>a partial aspect</note> or by the sight of cruel planets; or if it intends to make or is making an <term>Itshala<note>original: "Itthashala"; a Persian-derived term for a planetary application or aspect</note></term> with planets situated in the houses of loss, enemies, or death (12th, 6th, and 8th)—then it is powerless.

->The tail of Rahu <note>the South Node of the Moon, Ketu</note> situated in the Ascendant. || 71 ||<-

A planet is powerless [under these conditions]. Likewise, one situated in an enemy's sign is powerless. One situated in its <term>fall<note>original: "Nicha"; the sign where a planet's influence is weakest</note></term> is powerless. A planet desiring retrograde motion is powerless. A planet joined with "cruel" <note>malefic</note> planets is powerless. A planet that has "gone to setting" <note>combust, or too close to the Sun</note> is powerless. 

Furthermore, if there is a "Benefic Shila" <note>a configuration of harmony</note>, but it is formed with a planet that is malefic, in its sign of fall, or in an enemy's sign, that planet becomes powerless. Likewise, if it is seen by the "sight" <note>aspect</note> of malefic planets from the sixth, fourth, tenth, or seventh positions, it is powerless. Also, if it intends to form—or is currently forming—an *Itshala* <note>planetary application</note> with planets residing in the twelfth, sixth, or eighth houses, it is powerless. Furthermore, if a planet does not have a "view" of its own home <note>its ruling sign</note> from its current position—specifically the seventh house from it—then there is a loss of strength.

It notably incorporates "Yavana" (foreign/Perso-Arabic) terminology like Itthashala and Durapha into the Sanskrit astrological framework.</summary>

# Page 1
## Chapter 1

A planet is not strong if it is joined with a cruel <note>original: "krura," referring to malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</note> or sinful planet. Having thus spoken generally of planetary strength, the author now describes temporal strength <term>Samaya Bala: strength derived from the time of day or night</term>. This begins with the phrase "The Moon, Mars, and Mercury at evening..." and connects to the following verse.

### Verse 68
Now another method is described: Venus, the Moon, and Mars <note>original: "Sitendu-Bhaumah"</note> are strong when they rise at the time of the evening twilight. Jupiter and Saturn <note>original: "Jivarkajau," literally 'Jupiter and the son of the Sun'</note> are strong in the latter part of the night, specifically the period after midnight. Furthermore, the male planets—the Sun, Mars, and Jupiter—are strong during the day. Other planets—the Moon, Mercury, Venus, and Saturn—are also strong at night. Additionally, if planets are situated in the same portion <note>original: "eka-bhaga," likely referring to the same zodiacal sign or navamsha division</note> as the Sun at sunrise, they become strong. Therefore, the reading "others at night, resting in the same portion" is the better version. Others are strong at night if they rise in the latter part of the night; Jupiter and Saturn are powerful then. Others are strong at night when situated in the same portion as the Sun, and those in fixed signs are endowed with strength.

### Verse 69

-> Female planets are strong in the fourth house, or in even signs; male planets are strong in odd signs. <-
-> In other positions they are weak; having reflected on this, the specific results should be declared. <-

### Verse 70

---

There is also a reading that says "in the half-portion of the Sun." It was said by Samarasimha <note>A noted historical authority on Tajika or Perso-Arabic astrology in India</note>: "Planets are strong when situated in the same portion as the Sun." Regarding the interpretation of "in one portion" as being in the same sign or <term>Navamsha: a one-ninth division of a sign</term>, some argue this is possible even if the planet is combust <note>original: "astamitattva," rendered invisible by the Sun's proximity</note>. However, it is never heard that a combust planet is strong; on the contrary, it is heard by all in the texts that they are powerless <note>original: "alibanneva," likely a corruption of 'abalin' or 'weak'</note>. Therefore, the reading "in the same portion as the Sun" is correct. Furthermore, "fixed" <note>original: "sthira"</note> means that planets situated in the signs of Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius are endowed with strength—meaning they are very powerful.

### Verse 69 (Commentary)
Now other types are mentioned: "Women" etc. Female planets are strong when occupying the fourth house or the even signs <note>Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, Scorpio, Capricorn, Pisces</note>. This means they are strong when they are within the fourth house or its mid-point. Male planets...

<summary>This page details the specific conditions under which planets gain "temporal strength" based on the time of day and their placement in specific zodiac signs or houses. It includes a scholarly debate on whether planets near the Sun retain strength or become weakened by combustion.</summary>

<keywords>planetary strength, Samaya Bala, Samarasimha, male and female planets, evening strength, fixed signs, combustion, fourth house</keywords>

--- Page 74 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary yogas (combinations), specifically focusing on the Moon's strength and its aspects with Mars and Saturn in birth and inquiry charts.</meta>

Now the author describes another secondary result of the Moon. Regarding the "waning" Moon: the Moon's influence follows its phase. A waning Moon—meaning from the eighth day of the dark fortnight until the eighth day of the bright fortnight—is considered weak. <note>Some authorities define the waning period specifically from the eleventh day of the dark fortnight until the New Moon.</note> Thus, if the Moon is at the very end of a sign at night, it is not auspicious and yields harmful results. In this manner, the Moon should be carefully considered at the time of birth or at the time of a formal inquiry <term>Prashna: a branch of astrology where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked</term>. Furthermore, if Mars in the bright fortnight, or Saturn <note>original: "Arkasunu" or "Shanaishchara," both meaning the son of the Sun</note> in the dark fortnight, should behold the Moon through a fourth, tenth, seventh, or first house aspect, then that Moon is not auspicious and does not grant good results. || 74 ||

Now, he describes how even a favorable Moon can incur a flaw through a specific combination. Regarding the "bright" phase: in the bright fortnight, during the day, if the Moon is in a masculine sign <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, Libra, Sagittarius, or Aquarius</note>—

->The waning Moon at the end of a sign is not auspicious; thus should the Moon be considered at birth or inquiry. If Mars in the bright fortnight, or Saturn in the dark fortnight,<-
->beholds the bright Moon, it is not auspicious. || 74 || In the bright fortnight by day, if Saturn beholds the Moon in a masculine sign, or in the dark fortnight at night, if Mars<-
->beholds the Moon in a feminine sign, the flaw is lessened; otherwise, it is great. This should be weighed by the wise in inquiries and births. || 75 ||<-

->Thus end the Sixteen Yogas.<-

If Saturn <note>original: "Arkasuta"</note> beholds the Moon as described, it creates a "Duraphada" <note>A corruption of the Arabic/Persian astrological term 'Duffali' or 'Da'f', referring to planetary weakness</note>, but it spreads only a small amount of that flaw. Otherwise, if Saturn does not behold it in that specific way, the "Duraphada" flaw is spread in abundance. Similarly, in the dark fortnight at night, if Mars beholds the Moon while it is in a feminine sign <note>Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, Scorpio, Capricorn, or Pisces</note>, the flaw is lessened. If Mars does not behold it so, the flaw is abundant. All of this must be deliberated upon by the wise person in the context of an inquiry chart or a birth chart. That is to say, even the slightest detail must be considered. Thus, the Sixteen Yogas are completed. || 75 ||

<summary>This text concludes the explanation of the Sixteen Yogas, detailing how the Moon's auspiciousness is modified by its phase, the time of day, and aspects from Mars and Saturn. It emphasizes that specific planetary alignments can either mitigate or amplify astrological flaws (Duraphada).</summary>

# Page 38
## Chapter 3

The planet has become powerless. For example, if the sign occupied by a planet is its "soft" <note>original: *mṛdu*</note> home or its place of setting (combustion), then it is considered powerless. Likewise, regarding the tail of Rahu <note>the descending lunar node, Ketu</note> and its highest degrees: a planet that stands in the degrees of Rahu's tail or the degrees Rahu is yet to traverse is powerless. || 71 || Now, other types of weakness are described: "One who does not behold..." <note>original: *anīkṣamāṇa*</note>. A planet that does not behold (aspect) the Ascendant <note>the *Lagna* or first house</note> is powerless. Similarly, a planet situated in the setting portion—specifically, the seventh Navamsha <note>a ninth-part division of a sign</note> from the Navamsha where the Sun is rising—is powerless. This was also stated by Samarasimha: "Situated in the setting portion of the Sun." Furthermore, a planet that is devoid of its own "places" <note>dignities</note> such as its own house, its exaltation, its own *Hadda* <note>term/bound</note>, its own *Drekkana* <note>decan</note>, or its own *Navamsha* is powerless. Likewise, a planet that forms a *Krura-Isarapha* <note>a harsh or "evil" aspectual connection in Tajika astrology</note> with a malefic planet is not endowed with strength.

-> One who does not behold the body (Ascendant), who is in the setting portion, or is void of its own house and exaltation; <-
-> One in a harsh *Isarapha* is not endowed with vigor, and thus is not capable of accomplishing the task. || 72 || <-
-> The Moon is powerless in the twelfth house from the Sun, in the first half of Scorpio, and especially in the end of Libra. <-
-> When the Moon's form is not seen by the Lord of its sign, or it is in the "Empty Path," it is diminished. || 73 || <-

...is not endowed with vigor. Because that planet is not capable—not powerful enough—to bring about the desired task. || 72 || Having thus spoken generally of the weaknesses <note>original: *durappha*, likely referring to *Duhphali* or weak configurations</note> of all planets, he now describes the specific weaknesses regarding the Moon's station. "The Moon...": If the Moon is in the twelfth house from the sign occupied by the Sun, it is powerless. Likewise, in the first half of Scorpio <note>original: *vṛścika*</note>, it is destroyed and gives inauspicious results; it is powerless. Likewise, in the latter half of Libra <note>original: *tulā*</note>, it is powerless. Furthermore, if the Moon is not "seen" (aspected) by the Lord of the sign in which it is currently rising, then it is considered powerless by all planets. Likewise, if the Moon is in the "Empty Path" <note>original: *śūnyamārga*, similar to the "Void of Course" Moon</note> as characterized by the previously mentioned traits, it should be known as diminished and powerless. || 73 ||

<term>Isarapha</term> — A Persian-derived astrological term (Ittisala) used in Tajika astrology to describe the application or separation of planetary aspects.
<term>Navamsha</term> — A 1/9th division of a zodiac sign, used to determine the subtle strength of a planet.
<term>Empty Path</term> — A condition where the Moon makes no major aspects to other planets, indicating a lack of support for an undertaking.

<summary>This page details the specific astronomical conditions that make a planet "powerless" in Tajika astrology, including its relationship to the Sun, its lack of essential dignities, and specific debilities for the Moon such as the "Empty Path."</summary>

--- Page 76 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Tajika astrology from Neelkantha’s Sangya Tantra, specifically focusing on the calculation of planetary strength through 'Harsha-sthanas' (Joyful Positions). It follows the previous discussion on planetary aspects and configurations.</meta>

The total maximum strength is 20 <term>Vimshopakas</term> <note>A unit of measurement in Vedic astrology where 20 points represent a full unit of strength, similar to a score out of 20</note>. Within this, there are four "Positions of Joy" (<term>Harsha-sthanas</term>). Based on these specific indicators, each of the four positions is assigned 5 Vimshopakas; thus, by attaining all four positions, a planet gains the full 20 points of strength.

Here is an example: The Sun and other planets are situated in houses such as the 9th from the Ascendant. Therefore, the first Joyful Position was not obtained. In this case, Jupiter is in its own sign and Saturn is in its sign of exaltation; therefore, the second Joyful Position is obtained, granting a strength of 5 Vimshopakas. The female planets <note>In this system, Saturn, Venus, and Mercury are categorized as feminine or neutral-feminine for specific strength calculations</note> Saturn, Venus, and Mercury are in the 7th, 8th, and 9th houses from the Ascendant. The male planet, the Sun, stands in the 10th house. Thus, the third Joyful Position is obtained, also granting 5 Vimshopakas. This occurs during the day—

->
| Category | Sun | Moon | Mars | Merc. | Jup. | Ven. | Sat. |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| **House Pos.** | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 5 |
| **Exalt./Own** | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 5 |
| **Gender/Sec.** | 5 | 0 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 |
| **Day/Night** | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 | 5 |
| **Total Joy** | 10 | 0 | 5 | 5 | 10 | 5 | 15 |
<-

The jewel of the Garga lineage, the learned mathematician Chintamani, had a son named Ananta. From him, having destroyed ignorance, this method was received. His son, the wise Neelkantha, through the grace of the feet of Lord Vishveshvara <note>A title for Shiva, the patron deity of Varanasi</note>, described the sixteen planetary yogas and the Joyful Positions in this "Division of Definitions" (<term>Sangya-Viveka</term>). // 77 //

Thus ends the Chapter on the Sixteen Yogas.

---

At the moment of the Solar Return <note>original: "Varshapravesha"</note>, the male planets—Sun, Mars, and Jupiter—attain the fourth Joyful Position. Here too, the strength is 5 Vimshopakas. In this manner, the Joyful Positions for all planets and their respective strengths are written down. // 76 // 77 //

Thus ends the Chapter on the Sixteen Yogas, along with the commentary and examples, in the *Sangya Tantra* composed by the astrologer Neelkantha, as explained by Vishvanatha, the son of the astrologer Divakara.

# Now, the calculation of the Sahamas is described.

In this section, the fifty *Sahamas* <note>Arabic Parts or sensitive points in a horoscope</note> are explained in the first four verses. The term "Fortune" (*Punya*) is self-explanatory. || 1 || "Sickness" (*Maangha*) in the context of disease. || 2 || 3 || 4 ||

Now, the method for calculating the *Punya Sahama* <note>The Part of Fortune</note> is described: "The Sun does not..." etc. If the birth occurs during the day, the Sun is subtracted from the Moon and the result is added to the Ascendant. If at night, the Moon is subtracted from the Sun and added to the Ascendant. From this, the following are derived: 1. Fortune (*Punya*), 2. Knowledge (*Guru*), 3. Fame, 4. Wealth/Friendship, 5. Greatness, 6. Hope, 7. Capability, 8. Brother, 9. Mother, 10. Respect/Dignity, 11. Kingdom/Authority, 12. Father, 13. Mother <note>The text lists 'Mother' twice, likely referring to different maternal aspects or relatives</note>, 14. Son, 15. Life, 16. Water, 17. Action/Work. || 1 || 

18. Sickness (*Maangha*), 19. Love/Desire, 20. Conflict (*Kali*), then Forgiveness 21., 22. Action/Work, 23. Scripture/Science, 24. Relatives, 25. Self-expression/Speech. || 2 || 

26. Death, 27. Foreign Lands, 28. Wealth, 29. Other's Wife, 30. Other's Work, 31. All-encompassing, 32. Success in Work. || 2 || 

33. Marriage, 34. Memory, 35. Distress/Heat, 36. Faith, 36. Affection <note>The numbering repeats 36 in the original</note>, 37. Unsteadiness, 38. Body. 39. Dullness/Stupidity, 40. Business/Trade, 41. Falling into Water, 42. Enemy. || 3 || 

43. Valor, 44. Means/Strategy, 45. Poverty, 46. Teacher's Words, 47. Wisdom/Intellect, 48. Deception, 49. Daughter, 50. Mother-in-law. These are the fifty.

->Purified Planets<-
<note>These represent the calculated longitudinal positions of the planets used in the examples below</note>

| Sun | Moon | Mars | Merc | Jup | Ven | Sat | Asc |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 5 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 7 | 6 | 0 |
| 7 | 22 | 14 | 12 | 21 | 24 | 6 | 15 |
| 30 | 9 | 32 | 16 | 35 | 21 | 48 | 3 |
| 6 | 47 | 36 | 8 | 13 | 48 | 54 | 54 |

From these... || 4 || Subtracting the Sun from the Moon and adding the Ascendant for a day birth is called the *Punya Sahama*. The interval between the subtrahend and the minuend added to the Ascendant defines these points. || 5 ||

The Moon is to be subtracted. The result added to the Ascendant becomes the *Sahama* known as Fortune. Now, a special refinement for calculating the *Sahama* of the Year is mentioned: "If the Ascendant sign does not fall within the interval between the subtrahend and the minuend, then one full sign (30 degrees) must be added to the *Sahama*." For example, the sum...

<summary>This page lists the fifty Sahamas (astrological sensitive points) and provides the specific mathematical formula for calculating the Punya Sahama (Part of Fortune) for day and night births.</summary>
<keywords>Sahama, Punya Sahama, Tajik Astrology, Part of Fortune, planetary calculations, astrological formulas</keywords>

--- Page 78 ---
That which is subtracted is the "subtrahend," and that from which it is subtracted is the "minuend." This refers to the positions of the stars and zodiac signs. Specifically, the subtrahend planet is subtracted from the minuend planet. This process continues until the "base of the uncleared" <note>the remainder of the calculation</note> is reached. For example, if the planet to be subtracted is taken from the base planet, and the zodiac sign of the base planet is between the subtrahend and the minuend, then one full zodiac sign <note>30 degrees</note> must be added to the calculation of the *Fortuna Saham* <term>Saham: an Arabic astrological point or "Lot" used in Tajika Jyotisha</term>. If the sign does not fall between them, then no sign is added; this is established by the sense of the text. This rule for the minuend and subtrahend should also be understood to apply to the *Bhava Saham* <note>the Lot related to the astrological houses</note>. It is said here that because of the lack of clarity in the meaning, the *Fortuna* value is derived from the field, planet, and sign. These terms for the zodiac signs are to be known as markers for the addition process.

**Example:**
Sun: 1s 17° 30' 6"
Moon: 5s 22° 14' 47"
Subtracted (with additive constant): 8s 14° 13' 41"
Ascendant <term>Lagna: the rising sign on the eastern horizon</term>: 0s 18° 3' 54"
Summed together, the *Fortuna Saham* is: 5s 2° 17' 35" || 5 ||

Now, the method for calculating the *Sahams* of Jupiter <note>Wisdom/Wealth</note>, Knowledge, and Fame is stated: "In reverse..."

-> The calculation for Jupiter and Knowledge is the reverse of this; for Jupiter, subtract the *Fortuna* point. <-
-> In the day, it is reversed; in the night, it is as before; this they call the *Saham* of Fame. || 6 || <-

From this calculation of the *Fortuna Saham*, the calculation for the Jupiter and Knowledge *Sahams* is to be done in reverse. That is to say: during the day, the Moon is subtracted from the Sun, and the Ascendant is added. If the solar return <note>Varshapravesha</note> occurs at night, the Sun is subtracted from the Moon, and the Ascendant is added. These become the Jupiter and Knowledge *Sahams*. For the Jupiter *Saham* during the day, Jupiter <note>original: "Surejya," teacher of the gods</note> is subtracted from the *Fortuna Saham*. At night, it is the reverse: the *Fortuna Saham* is subtracted from Jupiter. As before, the Ascendant is added. The necessary corrections for the subtrahend and minuend must be applied to these *Sahams*. The wise call this the *Fame Saham*.

**Example:**
Sun: 1s 17° 30' 6"
Moon: 5s 22° 14' 47"
Subtracted (with additive constant): 3s 15° 20' 19"
Added to Ascendant: 4s 3° 24' 13"
This result is the Jupiter *Saham*: 5s 3° 24' 13"
This same value is the Knowledge *Saham*: 5s 3° 24' 13"
[And the] *Fortuna Saham*...

It emphasizes the "reverse" calculation rules that differ between day and night births in the Tajika system.</summary>

2 | 24 | 31 | 35 subtracted from Jupiter 8 | 21 | 35 | 32 leaves a remainder of 1 | 12 | 55 | 38. When added to the Ascendant, the **Lot of Fame** <note>original: Yashas-saham; a point representing reputation and glory</note> is produced: 0 | 14 | 55 | 32. || 6 ||

Now he describes the calculation for the **Lot of Friendship** <note>original: Mitra-saham</note> || "Called Merit..." || During the day, one should subtract the **Lot of Merit** <note>original: Punya-saham; often equivalent to the Lot of Fortune</note> from the position of Jupiter. At night, it is the reverse: subtract Jupiter from the Lot of Merit. Then, add the position of Venus <note>original: Sita/Shukra</note>. In this specific calculation, the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> is not added. Because Venus is added, the method of "adding one" <note>term: Saikata; a technical adjustment in Tajika astrology where 1 sign is added if the planet to be subtracted does not lie between the Ascendant and the other planet</note> should be performed as previously described for the body. That rule is as follows: The wise who know the truth understand the Lot of Friendship to be found by the "adding one" method if Venus does not fall within the interval of the subtrahend and the starting point. Example: Lot of Merit 2 | 24 | 31 | 35 subtracted from Jupiter 5 | 32 | 24 | 32 leaves a remainder of 8 | 7 | 50 | 38. Adding Venus 7 | 24 | 32 | 48 and adding one—

->Subtract the Lot of Merit from Jupiter; at night, the reverse. Add Venus.<-
->The wise know the Lot of Friendship by the "adding one" method,<-
->just as described for the body. || 7 || Subtract the degrees of the Lot of Merit as stated;<-
->there the Lot of Greatness is found. From Saturn, during the day, and at night<-
->the reverse, subtract Venus for the Lot of Hope, as I have stated. || 8 || || ||<-

—the **Lot of Friendship** is produced: 4 | 25 | 22 | 26. || Now he describes the calculation for the **Lot of Greatness** <note>original: Mahatmya-saham</note> and the **Lot of Hope** <note>original: Asha-saham</note>. || "From Merit..." || One should subtract Mars <note>original: Manda/Bhauma; though 'Manda' usually refers to Saturn, the commentary clarifies the calculation using Mars</note> from the Lot of Merit during the day; then the addition of the Ascendant and the "adding one" rule should be applied. At night, this is reversed: subtract the Lot of Merit from Mars. That shall be the Lot of Greatness. Now, for the Lot of Hope: during the day, subtract Venus from Saturn. At night, the reverse: Saturn should be subtracted from Venus. The addition of the Ascendant and the "adding one" rule should be applied as previously stated. That will be the Lot of Hope or the **Lot of Desire** <note>original: Iccha-saham</note>. Example: Mars 8 | 24 | 32 | 36 subtracted from the Lot of Merit 9 | 24 | 43 | 35 leaves a remainder of 0 | 18 | 10 | 59. Adding the Ascendant and adding one, the **Lot of Greatness** is produced: 2 | 6 | 19 | 53. For Saturn 6 | 8 | 46 | 54 and Venus 7 | 24 | 32 | 48.

# Now, the calculation for the Sahama of Capability and the Sahama of Brothers.

Regarding **Capability** <note>Samarthya</note>: Subtract the Lord of the Ascendant from Mars <note>Bhauma</note> during the day. At night, subtract Mars from the Lord of the Ascendant. Add the degrees of the Ascendant as previously described; this becomes the **Sahama of Capability**. If the Lord of the Ascendant happens to be Mars itself, what should be done? The author says: If Mars is the Lord of the Ascendant, one should always subtract it from Jupiter <note>Guru</note>, both during the day and at night; that becomes the Sahama of Capability. Now, for the **Sahama of Brothers** <note>Bhratrusahama</note>: subtract Saturn <note>Arki/Shani</note> from the [calculated point], and as before, with the previous adjustments, it becomes the Sahama of Brothers. 

**Example:** To subtract the Lord of the Ascendant from Mars. Here, the Lord of the Ascendant is Mars. For this reason, [subtract] from the position of Jupiter: 8;21;5;36.

-> To find Capability and Brothers, subtract the Lord of the Ascendant inversely; but if the Lord is Mars, subtract from Jupiter always. As before, for Brothers, subtract Saturn from the day-point. || 9 || By day, subtract the Moon from Jupiter and add the Sun; in reverse, for the Kingly/Fatherly point, subtract the Sun from Saturn or vice versa at night. || 110 || <-

From Mars: 8;24;32;36. The remainder after subtraction is 0;15;26;37. When added to the Ascendant, it becomes the **Sahama of Capability**: 2;3;6;31. From Jupiter: 8;21;5;36. From Saturn: 6;5;14;8;54. The remainder after subtraction is 2;22;46;19. When added to the Ascendant, it becomes the **Sahama of Brothers**: 4;10;15;13. || 9 ||

# Now, the calculation for the Sahama of Respect and the Sahama of the Royal Father.

Regarding **By Day**: During the daytime, the Moon should be subtracted from Jupiter, and the Sun should be added. At night, the Sun should be subtracted from Jupiter and the Moon should be added. Following the stated method, including the addition of a sign <note>saitaka-chakra</note> if the subtraction is reversed, this becomes the **Sahama of Respect** <note>Gauravasahama</note>. That is to say: between the subtrahend and the minuend, in order of day and night, the Sun and Moon are placed in the Ascendant... <insert>meaning the addition of a sign is performed</insert>. By day, [to find] the Kingly point, subtract the Sun from Saturn; at night, do the opposite, subtracting Saturn from the Sun...

# Now, the calculation for the Sahamas of Action, Disease, and Desire

Regarding the "Knower of Action" <note>original: *Karmajnam*; referring to the Karma Sahama or the Lot of Action/Work</note>: During the day, Mercury is to be subtracted from Mars. At night, the reverse: Mars is to be subtracted from Mercury. The Ascendant <note>the rising sign</note> is added to this difference, and one is added <note>the 'Saitaka' or 'plus one' rule used when the Ascendant does not fall between the two planets</note>; this becomes the **Karma Sahama** (Lot of Action). For the **Roga Sahama** (Lot of Disease), at all times—whether day or night—the Moon is to be subtracted from the Ascendant. The Ascendant is then added to this result along with one; this becomes the Lot of Disease. For the **Manmatha Sahama** (Lot of Desire/Cupid), during the day, the Lord of the Ascendant is to be subtracted from the Moon. At night, the reverse: the Moon is to be subtracted from the Lord of the Ascendant. The Ascendant is added to this result along with one; this becomes the Lot of Desire, also known as Madana. Alternatively, for both day and night, if one subtracts the Moon from the Lord of the Ascendant and adds the Ascendant plus one, it becomes the Lot of Desire.

**Example:** When Mercury is subtracted from Mars, the remainder is 0s 21° 6' 28". Adding the Ascendant and one, the Karma Sahama becomes 1s 20° 20' 22". When the Moon is subtracted from the Ascendant...

> The Knower of Action (Mercury) from Mars; at night the reverse is said. The Lot of Disease is always from the Moon subtracted from the Ascendant. || 11 ||
> Desire (Manmatha) is the Lord of the Ascendant from the Moon by day; the reverse at night: the Moon from the Lord of the Ascendant. || 12 ||
> Kalishma <note>a term likely referring to a specific type of suffering or sin</note> is from Jupiter to Mars; if at night, Mars to Jupiter, following the previous method.
> For the Shastra (Knowledge/Scripture) Lot, subtract Saturn from Jupiter by day; at night, the reverse from Mercury, adding the sum as before. || 13 ||

...the Moon subtracted from the Ascendant leaves a remainder of 6s 25° 54' 7". Adding the Ascendant, the Roga Sahama is 7s 13° 58' 11". The Lord of the Ascendant (Mars in this case) subtracted from the Moon leaves a remainder of 9s 7° 37' 11". Adding the Ascendant, the Madana Sahama is 2s 25° 44' 15". || 12 ||

### Now, the calculation for the Sahamas of Kalishma and Shastra

Regarding **Kalishma**: During the day, Mars is to be subtracted from Jupiter. Conversely, at night, Jupiter is to be subtracted from Mars. Following the previous method, the Ascendant is added plus one; this becomes the Kalishma Sahama. For the **Shastra Sahama** (Lot of Scripture/Science): During the day, Saturn is to be subtracted from Jupiter. At night, the reverse is subtracted from Mercury <note>original: *Jnasya*; referring to Mercury</note>. The sum is added as before. If the Ascendant does not fall within the interval, one is added. This becomes the Shastra Sahama.

**Example:** Mars subtracted from Jupiter leaves a remainder of 0s 15° 2' 37". Adding the Ascendant and one, the Kalishma Sahama is produced...

-> Subtracting the Sun from Venus gives the Adultery point; always as before. For Other Works, <-
-> subtract Saturn from the Moon, or the reverse at night. For <-
-> Commerce, use the method of the Sun and Moon. || 16 || Subtract the Sun from Saturn by day, and the Moon from Saturn at night; adding the lord of the Sun or Moon <-
-> results in the Point of Success. Always subtracting Saturn from Venus gives the Marriage point. || 17 || || <-

If the year begins during the day or night, subtract Mercury from the Moon, add the Ascendant and add one; this is the **Commerce Saham** <note>Vanika Saham</note>. 

**Example:** Subtracting the Sun from Venus, the remainder is 10 | 17 | 2 | 42. Adding the Ascendant, the **Adultery Saham** is 1 | 15 | 5 | 36. Subtracting Mercury from the Moon, the remainder is 9 | 15 | 5 | 39. Adding the Ascendant, the **Commerce Saham** is 8 | 27 | 57 | 33. || 16 ||

Now, the calculation for the **Point of Success** <note>Karya-siddhi Saham</note> and the **Marriage Point** <note>Vivaha Saham</note> is explained. "From Saturn," etc. In the daytime, subtract the Sun from Saturn and add the lord of the Sun's sign. At night, subtract the Moon from Saturn and add the lord of the Moon's sign. Add one; this becomes the **Success Saham**. Always, whether the year begins by day or night, subtract Saturn from Venus <insert>to find the Marriage point</insert>.

### Calculation Example
From the position of Saturn, subtract the Sun; the resulting difference is 8 signs, 22 degrees, 18 minutes, and 8 seconds. Since Saturn is the lord of the Sun's sign, by adding this to the Ascendant, the **Success Sahama** becomes 2s 6° 7' 42". 
Subtracting Venus from the Moon results in a difference of 2s 17° 4' 54". When added to the Ascendant, the **Marriage Sahama** becomes 2s 5° 46' 48". || 17 ||

### On the Sahamas of Childbirth and Distress
Now the method for bringing forth the **Birth** and **Distress Sahamas** is described. 
**Regarding Jupiter:** During the day, subtract Mercury from Jupiter. At night, it is the reverse: subtract Jupiter from Mercury. 
By adding the Ascendant and applying the "plus one" rule <note>original: "saikata," referring to the addition of a degree under specific conditions of planetary aspect</note>, the **Birth Sahama** <term>Prasuti Sahama: A mathematical point relating to labor and delivery</term> is produced. 

For the **Distress Sahama** <term>Santapta Sahama: A point indicating grief or burning anxiety</term>, always subtract the Moon from Saturn, whether by day or night. Add the value of the Sixth House <note>original: "shashti-kshepa," likely referring to the cusp of the 6th house of illness/enemies</note> and follow the previously mentioned rules. This relates to the influence of Mars and Venus.

### Calculation Example
Subtracting Mercury from Jupiter gives a difference of 0s 27° 12' 5". 
<margin>Subtract Mercury from Jupiter for Birth; at night, reverse it. Subtract Moon from Saturn for Distress.</margin>
By adding the Ascendant and the "plus one," the **Birth Sahama** is 2s 5° 22' 59". 
Subtracting the Moon from Saturn gives 0s 24° 12' 17". Adding the value of the "Enemy House" <note>The 6th House</note> (5s 13° 45' 16") and adding one, the **Distress Sahama** is 6s 28° 42' 3". || 18 ||

### On the Sahamas of Faith, Love, and Strength
Now the calculation for **Faith**, **Love**, and the **Valadeva** <note>Strength/Power</note> Sahamas is described. 
**Faith:** Always (day or night) subtract Mars from Venus. By adding the Ascendant and the "plus one" rule, the **Faith Sahama** <term>Shraddha Sahama</term> is formed. 
**Love:** Always subtract the **Fortune Sahama** <note>Punya Sahama</note> from the **Vidha Sahama** <note>A point related to understanding or action</note>. Following the previous method of adding the Ascendant, the **Love Sahama** <term>Priti Sahama</term> is formed. 

Next are the Sahamas for **Merit** and **Fame**, specifically the one called **Valadeva** <note>Named after the deity of strength</note>. This refers to "Bala" (military force or personal capacity). One must discard "Jadya" (sluggishness or ignorance) and look to the position of Mars and the Ascendant.

...subtracting from Saturn, and adding one <note>The "plus one" rule is a common adjustment in Tajik astrology when the planet to be subtracted has a higher longitude than the starting point</note>. **Example:** Subtracting the mean position of Mars from the starting point gives 2 Signs, 7 Degrees, 43 Minutes, 42 Seconds. Now, the calculation for the **Poverty** <note>Daridra</note> and **Greatness/Jupiter** <note>Guruta</note> Sahamas is described:

By day, subtract mean Saturn from Jupiter; by night, subtract mean Jupiter from Saturn. Add the Ascendant and one; this shall be the **Greatness Sahama**.
By day, subtract Mercury from the Fortune Sahama and add Mercury; by night, subtract the Fortune Sahama from Mercury and add Mercury. Add one; this shall be the **Poverty Sahama**.
By day, subtract the Sun from the Sun’s Apogee <note>highest point in its orbit</note>; by night, subtract the Moon from the Moon’s Apogee. Following the previous method of adding the Ascendant...

> Subtracting Mercury from Fortune by night, and the reverse by day, adding Mercury, is Poverty. Subtracting the Sun from its Apogee, or the Moon from its Apogee by night, as previously stated... || 22 ||
> From half of Cancer (3 Signs, 15 Degrees), subtract Saturn for the Water-path <note>Jalapatha</note>; otherwise by night. Subtracting Saturn from Fortune by day, and the reverse by night, is Bondage <note>Bandhana</note>. || 23 ||

...and adding one shall be the Greatness Sahama. **Example:** Subtracting Jupiter from mean Saturn gives 9 | 7 | 32 | 41. Adding the Ascendant and one, the result is 9 | 8 | 56 | 36.

Subtracting the Sun from the Sun’s Apogee gives 3 | 2 | 29 | 54. Adding the Ascendant and one, the **Greatness Sahama** is 4 | 20 | 39 | 48. || 22 ||

Now, the calculation for the **Water-path** <term>Jalapatha: relates to voyages or journeys by water</term> and **Bondage** <term>Bandhana: relates to imprisonment or constraints</term> Sahamas is described:

By day, from half of Cancer (which is 3 Signs and 15 Degrees), subtract Saturn. Otherwise, by night, subtract 3 Signs and 15 Degrees from mean Saturn. Add the Ascendant and one; this shall be the Water-path Sahama.
By day, subtract Saturn from the Fortune Sahama; by night, subtract Fortune from Saturn...

# Page 15

The <term>Saham of Fortune</term> <note>Sanskrit: Punya-saham; a sensitive point representing prosperity and luck</note> should be purified, then the Ascendant is added, and one should be added to the sum; that becomes the **Saham of Wealth** <note>Dhana-saham</note>.

### Example
When Saturn is subtracted from three and a half signs (3s 15°), the remainder is 9s 8° 11' 6". When the Ascendant is added, the **Saham of Fortune** becomes 9s 26° 15' 0". When Saturn is subtracted from the Saham of Fortune, the remainder is 2s 25° 56' 41". When the Ascendant is added and one is added to that, the **Saham of Wealth** becomes 4s 14° 0' 35".

|| 23 ||

Now he describes the calculation for the **Saham of Virgins** <note>Kanya-saham</note> and the **Saham of Horses** <note>Ashva-saham</note>. Regarding the Moon: whether by day or night, the Moon should be subtracted from Venus. As stated before, the Ascendant is added and one is added; that becomes the Saham of Virgins. 

During the day, the Sun should be subtracted from the Saham of Fortune. At night, the Saham of Fortune should be subtracted from the Sun. The eleventh house <note>the house of gains</note> is added, and one is added; that becomes the Saham of Horses.

### Example
When the Moon is subtracted from Venus, the remainder is 2s 2° 22' 1". When the Ascendant is added and one is added, the **Saham of Virgins** becomes 3s 20° 25' 55". When the Sun is subtracted from the Saham of Fortune, the remainder is 1s 12° 51' 32" 19"'. When added to the eleventh house (10s 12° 6' 32")...

-> Subtract the Moon from Venus; this is always called the Saham of Virgins. Subtract the Sun from Fortune [by day], or the reverse at night, and add the house of gains. <-
-> || 24 || <-

...the **Saham of Horses** becomes 10s 14° 40' 7". 

Regarding these two Sahams, there is a lack of consensus because it was not mentioned by Samarasingha <note>A famous 13th-century authority on Tajika astrology</note>. In the *Samjna-Tantra* <note>A foundational technical manual</note>, it is stated that if the Ascendant does not fall between the minuend and the subtrahend, then one sign should be added to the Saham. However, a supporting statement for this is not found elsewhere. Even in the opinion of the Greeks <note>Yavanas</note>, the addition of one is not present. 

There is a variant reading: "from the second house" instead of "not unique." In this reading, the addition of one occurs. The author here has stated the addition of one for the Saham by adopting the reading "from the second house." However, in the works of Samarasingha, as well as in the *Yavana-Tajika* and the *Manushya-Tajika*, the reading is "from the Ascendant and the second house." In that reading, the addition of one does not occur. Consequently, all the great teachers have not mentioned the addition of one for the Saham. 

Here is an example as stated by Samarasingha: "During the day, from the portion of the Sun to be traversed, having combined the night passed..." <note>This quote explains the calculation of the Part of Fortune based on the arc between the Sun and Moon</note>. If there is an interval of signs between them, then...

<page-num>46</page-num>
<header>Chapter 1 — Page 46</header>

The degrees already traversed <note>original: "bhuktāṃśāḥ"</note>. Among the intermediate values, the degrees yet to be traversed <note>original: "bhogyāṃśakāḥ"</note> must be maintained. When thirty is subtracted from each house starting from the Ascendant <note>the "Lagna" or 1st house</note> or the second house, wherever the calculation comes to rest, that is where the **Punya Saham** <note>The Arabic-derived "Point of Fortune" used in Tajika astrology</note> is said to be located. 

If the solar return or entry occurs during the day, one should set aside the Sun's sign and place the degrees and minutes. The degrees should be subtracted from thirty, and the minutes from sixty. Whatever remains is the "portion to be traversed." To this, one should add the degrees and minutes of the Moon's sign. Following this, count the number of signs from the sign following the Sun's position up to the sign preceding the Moon's position; that number of signs (converted to degrees) should be added to the previously calculated degrees. 

Then, for each sign, calculate thirty degrees. Add these to the degrees placed below. After this, if the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> falls between the Sun's sign and the Moon's sign, then the degrees of the Ascendant's sign should be added. If the Ascendant does not fall between them, then the Ascendant's "portion to be traversed" should be subtracted. 

Furthermore, when the traversed degrees are used, the counting starts from the Ascendant. When the Ascendant's "portion to be traversed" is subtracted, the counting starts from the House of Wealth <note>the 2nd House</note>. By calculating in this manner and subtracting thirty degrees for each sign, the sign in which the final thirty is subtracted is known as the sign of the **Punya Saham**. In the reading "from the second house from the Ascendant," if the Ascendant is between the Sun and Moon, or even if it is not, the counting starts from the House of Wealth and not from the Ascendant.

->**Example**<-

| Planet/Point | Sign | Degree | Minute | Second |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sun** | 1 | 7 | 30 | 6 |
| **Moon** | 5 | 22 | 51 | 47 |
| **Ascendant (Lagna)** | 0 | 18 | 35 | 54 |

The Sun's portion to be traversed is 22;29,54. The Moon's traversed degrees are 22;51,47. Added together, they equal 45;21,41. The signs between the Sun and Moon are added, resulting in the sum 3;24;39;41. In total degrees, this is 104;39,41. 

Because the Ascendant is not between the Sun and the Moon, the Ascendant's traversed degrees (18;35,54) are added, resulting in 123;43,25. Subtracting thirty for each house starting from the Ascendant, the **Punya Saham** falls in the sign of Leo <note>the 5th sign</note> at 4;24;43,35. If subtracting thirty for each house starting from the House of Wealth, the **Punya Saham** falls in the sign of Virgo <note>the 6th sign</note> at 5;24;43,35. 

In this way, the calculation is arrived at. Thus, there is a dispute between the two readings. In the reading "from the second house," this is the logic: when the Ascendant's traversed degrees are added, the Ascendant remains as the reference point. For that reason, the Ascendant remains... <note>text cuts off</note>
