<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological calculations, specifically focusing on the timing of results for Sahamas (astrological lots or points). It references a previous discussion on the relationship between the Ascendant and the signs.</meta>

<page-num>47</page-num>
<header>Chapter 2</header>

When the degrees to be enjoyed <note>original: "svabhogyāṃśāḥ"; refers to the remaining portion of a zodiac sign yet to be traversed by a planet</note> are subtracted, then there is no connection with the Ascendant <note>term: "Lagna" — the rising sign on the eastern horizon at the time of birth or an event</note>. For this reason, there is no progression through two houses. Here, in the portion of the Ascendant, there is not even a row; such is the logic. Because there is no consensus among all authorities, the reading "not from the second house" is also found. And so it is said in the *Manushya Jataka* <note>A classical Sanskrit treatise on human horoscopy</note>: "From the portion to be enjoyed, these are taken in reverse until all are included from the other side, either in the Ascendant or from the Ascendant, or including the Ascendant when the portion to be enjoyed is situated within. Having made the sign... from the Ascendant and from the second, and from that 'Fortune' <note>term: "Punya"; likely referring to the Punya Sahama or Lot of Fortune</note>, the Sahamas are formed there, because the cessation is by degrees." || 24 ||

Now, having described the origin of the Sahamas, and intending to describe their results, the author addresses the question: "How many days after the commencement of the year <note>term: "Varshapravesha" — the solar return or the moment the Sun enters the same position as at birth</note> will the results of the Sahama be obtained?" He explains the method for calculating those days of attainment: "Subtract its own lord," and so on. For whichever Sahama one desires to know the days of attainment of good or bad results, that Sahama (expressed in signs, degrees, etc.) is the object to be acted upon. It should be subtracted by its own lord <note>the planet that rules the sign in which the Sahama falls</note>. The remainder should be converted into degrees. By those [degrees]—

-> Subtract its own lord from that Sahama; multiply those degrees by its own rising time, <-
-> and divide by ninety. The result is the 'ripening' in days, etc., occurring in its period or otherwise. <- || 25 ||

The degrees of the sign occupied by that Sahama are multiplied by its own rising time <note>term: "Udaya" — the time it takes for a specific zodiac sign to rise at a particular latitude</note> and divided by ninety <note>original: "trirāśyaṃśe" — literally 'the degrees of three signs'</note>. The resulting days are the "ripening" <note>term: "Paka" — the fruition or manifestation of astrological effects</note> of the Sahamas; that is, the time when the results mature. 

In this regard, by the elapsed days—using thirty as a base—from the time of the year's commencement, the longitude of the Sun is calculated. When the Sun has traversed that much, it becomes the time for the ripening of the results of that Sahama. Some, however, say that the ripening of results occurs after that many calculated days from the start of the major period <note>term: "Mahadasha"</note> of the lord of the Sahama, either in that period or another. 

Now, if the Sahama and its lord have the same position in signs and degrees, there would be no difference between them, and thus no "Sahama days" would be obtained. How then would the day of the Sahama's result be known? To this, he says: "Or in the impossibility of that." It is true; if it is impossible because there is no difference and no days are obtained, then the attainment of the Sahama's result will occur simply within the period <note>term: "Dasha"</note> of that Sahama.

<summary>This page explains the mathematical formula for determining when the specific "lots" (Sahamas) in a yearly horoscope will manifest their effects, primarily by calculating the distance between the lot and its planetary ruler. It also provides a solution for cases where the lot and its ruler occupy the same position.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Tajika astrology, specifically focusing on the timing of results for the Sahams (sensitive points) and the strength of their ruling planets.</meta>

The conclusion is that even there, the precise knowledge of time should be determined through the sub-periods <note>Antardasha</note> or the minor sub-periods <note>Vidasha</note>.

### Example

The <term>Punya Saham</term> <note>The 'Lot of Fortune' or 'Point of Merit' in Tajika astrology</note> is at 5 signs, 5 degrees, 25 minutes, and 1 second. Subtracting its own lord, Mercury (at 3 signs, 6 degrees, 14 minutes, and 12 seconds), the remainder is 2 signs, 29 degrees, 10 minutes, and 49 seconds. Converting this to total minutes of arc <note>Amshansha</note> gives 591 degrees, 10 minutes, and 49 seconds. Since the Saham is located in Virgo, multiplying by the rising time <note>Udaya</note> of Virgo (335), we get 191,825 minutes and 35 seconds. Dividing by thirty (300), the resulting time is 63 days, 5 <term>Ghatis</term> <note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>, and 4 <term>Palas</term> <note>A unit of time equal to 24 seconds</note>. After this many days have passed from the <term>Varshapravesha</term> <note>The moment the Sun returns to its natal position; the solar return</note>, the <term>Phalapaka</term> <note>The ripening or manifestation of results</note> of the Punya Saham occurs.

Alternatively, if the major period <note>Mahadasha</note> of Mercury, the lord of the Punya Saham, lasts for many days, the result of the Punya Saham ripens after this many days have passed from the start of Mercury’s major period. If the duration is short, then the lord of the Saham is considered. || 25 ||

Now, the results of the Sahams are explained. Regarding the "Exaltation and so on": The <term>Sahamapa</term> <note>The planetary lord of a specific Saham</note>, being the lord of the Saham's sign, is considered powerful if it is in its own exaltation, its own house, its own term <note>Hadda</note>, its own decan <note>Drekkana</note>, its own Navamsha <note>Varga division</note>, or a friendly sign. Or, if it is placed in a favorable house from the ascendant.

->If the lord of the Saham is in its exaltation or other favorable positions, and if it aspects the Ascendant, it is endowed with strength. If the lord of the Saham does not aspect the Ascendant, it is not considered powerful.<-
->The lords of the signs occupied by the Sun and Moon, and the lords of the Syzygy <note>New or Full Moon</note> points, should be judged for strength in this same way.<- || 26 ||

By implication, if it is in a friend's house or aspected by a friend or a benefic planet, and if it aspects the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note>, then it is "endowed with strength," meaning it is possessed of power and vigor. If the lord of the Saham, even while in its exaltation and so on, does not aspect the Ascendant, then it is not powerful, meaning it is weak.

Regarding "The Sun and Moon": This refers to the lords of the signs occupied by the Sun and the Moon. For example: the lord of the sign where the Sun was placed at the time of birth, and the lord of the sign where the Moon was placed at the time of birth. Furthermore, one should calculate the Ascendant for the time of the New Moon <note>Amavasya</note>—whether past or approaching—relative to the birth time, and find its lord. Similarly, find the lord of the Ascendant for the Full Moon <note>Purnanta</note> prior to birth. The strength of these five "Points of Merit" <note>Punya-bala</note> is to be judged in this manner. In the <term>Hillaj</term> <note>A reference to Al-Hajaj or a specific Persian/Arabic astrological tradition integrated into Sanskrit Tajika texts</note>, the longevity...

<meta>This page continues a technical commentary on astrological calculations, specifically focusing on the strength of planets and the "Sahama" (Arabic parts/lots) system used in Tajika astrology.</meta>

<header>48</header>

The use of evaluating the strength of these planets is applied in the calculation of journeys <note>original: "yanayane"</note> and in the calculation of the lifespan in human horoscopy. Since there is no immediate need for that here, I will not prolong the discussion unnecessarily. || 26 ||

Now, he describes the characteristics of weak and strong planets. Regarding the "Five-fold Strength" <note>term: "Panchavarga" - a system in Tajika astrology used to determine planetary strength based on five criteria: House, Exaltation, Bounds/Terms, Drekkana, and Navamsha</note>: the five-fold divisions have been mentioned previously. If a planet does not occupy any of the "Joyous Positions" <note>term: "Harshasthana" - specific locations in a chart where a planet gains "joy" or extra strength</note> such as the "Nanda-Tripadi" and others mentioned before, and if it does not aspect <note>look toward</note> the Ascendant <note>term: "Lagna" - the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</note> and other key points, then this planet is to be known as "immobile" or weak. If it resides in even a "minor position" <note>a specific subdivision of strength</note>, it is strong. For instance: when a planet is in its own house or its sign of exaltation, it has "Great Authority." When a planet is in its own "Bounds" <note>original: "Hadda" - a division of a sign similar to the Egyptian Terms</note>, it has "Medium Authority." When it is in its own "Face" <note>original: "Drekkan/Mukha"</note> or other minor divisions, it has "Minor Authority." Therefore, the wise have stated the gradation of strength: "Thirty points for its own sign, twenty for its exaltation," and so on.

-> The planet is strong if it possesses strength through the Five-fold divisions, occupies an excellent Joyous Position, and aspects the Ascendant, even if it is in a minor position. || 27 || <-

-> If a Lot <note>term: "Sahama" - sensitive points in a chart calculated by adding and subtracting the longitudes of planets and house cusps; a technique borrowed from Arabic astrology</note> is joined with or aspected by its own lord or by benefic planets, and if that lord is strong, then there is an increase/prosperity of that Lot. If it is not joined with or aspected by its lord or by auspicious planets, its manifestation will not occur; this should be considered first. || 28 || <-

If it aspects the Ascendant while situated in a minor position, such as its own Face or Bounds, then the planet is known as strong. || 27 || Having thus understood the strength and weakness of the lordship of the Lots, he now describes the increase or decrease of the Lot itself. "By its own lord...": if a Lot is joined with or aspected by its own ruler, or joined with or aspected by benefic <note>auspicious</note> planets, and if the lord of that Lot is strong according to the previously mentioned methods, then that Lot will flourish. If it lacks strength, it will be incapable of giving results. If a Lot is not aspected by its own lord or by benefic planets, its fruit is destroyed; in that case, the "possibility" of that Lot—meaning its capacity to produce results—will not exist. This means it does not give results corresponding to its name <note>e.g., a "Lot of Wealth" would not produce wealth</note>. This is the first thing to be contemplated and investigated. || 28 ||

### On the Signs of a Sahama’s Failure to Manifest Results

Now he describes the characteristics of a Sahama that cannot produce results. He says: "By the eighth..." — If a <term>Sahama</term> <note>A specific mathematical point in the horoscope, often called an 'Arabic Part' or 'Lot'</note> is joined with or aspected by its own lord or by the lord of the eighth house; or if it is joined with or aspected by a malefic planet; or if it forms an <term>Ithashala</term> <note>original: "shalyuthashila" - a Tajika aspect representing the application of planetary influence</note> with those malefic planets or the lord of the eighth house—then even if the Sahama is associated with its own lord or with benefic planets, the possibility of obtaining the Sahama's fruit undergoes a transformation. This means the Sahama does not give its intended result. For this reason, at the time of one's birth, one should first examine the strength and weakness of the Sahama and deliberate upon it. // 29 //

Now, regarding the determination of a strong Sahama and the rejection of a weak one, he says: "In the beginning..." — In the beginning, at the time of birth, after determining the strength and weakness of all fifty Sahamas, one should decide which ones are capable of producing results.

---

-> By the lord of the eighth, joined or aspected, or joined/aspected by malefics through Ithashala; its manifestation goes to destruction. Therefore, let this be examined by the experts at birth. // 29 // In the beginning, having reflected on the strength and weakness of all Sahamas at birth, one should determine those that are capable [of results]. // 30 // When the <term>Lot of Fortune</term> is strong, there is success in religious merit and the arrival of wealth; but if aspected by the lord of the <term>Lot of Expenditure</term>, the wise know this as the destruction of wealth. // 31 // <-

---

One should determine those capable of giving results. This means that those Sahama lords found to be weak in every way should never be considered as capable of producing results. // 30 // Having thus spoken of the Sahamas' ability to give results when they are strong, the fruits of all the Sahamas are now described. Among them, he first describes the fruit of the <term>Lot of Fortune</term> <note>original: "Punya-sahama" - literally the 'Lot of Merit/Virtue', equivalent to the Arabic 'Pars Fortunae'</note>.

When the <term>Lot of Fortune</term> is strong and is aspected by or joined with benefic planets and its own lord, there is the accomplishment of <term>Dharma</term> <note>religious or moral duty</note> and the arrival of wealth. If the <term>Lot of Expenditure</term> <note>original: "Vyaya-sahama"</note> is weak <insert>or if the Lot of Fortune is</insert> joined with or aspected by malefic planets, those who know the results say there is the destruction of merit and the loss of wealth. // 31 //

<summary>This page outlines the astrological conditions—such as malefic aspects or connections to the eighth house—that nullify the positive effects of a Sahama (Arabic Part). It specifically defines the auspicious results of a strong Lot of Fortune and the negative impact of a weak or afflicted Lot of Expenditure.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the 'Punya Saham' (Lot of Fortune/Virtue) within a Tajika-style annual horoscope (Varshaphala). It follows previous discussions on how to calculate and interpret specific sensitive points in the yearly chart.</meta>

# Page 1 / 49

<header>Decorative floral border band at the top of the page</header>

Now, the author describes the bad results along with the exceptions. **From the Ascendant:** When the *Punya Saham* <term>Punya Saham: A specific sensitive point in a yearly horoscope representing the 'Lot of Virtue' or 'Fortune'</term> is located in the eighth or twelfth houses from the Year-Ascendant <term>Varshalagna: The zodiac sign rising at the exact moment the sun returns to its birth position each year</term>, it becomes a destroyer of religion, fortune, and fame. However, if this point, while situated in those houses, is aspected <note>aspected: In astrology, when a planet 'looks' at a point from a specific distance, influencing its quality</note> by a benefic planet <term>Subha-graha: Auspicious planets like Jupiter or Venus</term> or by its own planetary lord, then in the end, happiness, religious merit, and similar gains will occur. This implies that the first half of the year would yield inauspicious results. || 32 ||

Now, he describes the results of aspects not previously mentioned regarding the connection between malefic and benefic planets. **Joined with a Malefic:** If that *Punya Saham* is joined with a malefic planet <term>Papa-graha: Inauspicious planets like Saturn or Mars</term> but is aspected by benefic planets, then the first half of the year will be inauspicious, while the second half of the year will be auspicious. Conversely, when the *Punya Saham* is joined with a benefic but aspected by a malefic, it will be auspicious in the beginning (the first half of the year) and bad or inauspicious in the latter part. If it is both joined with a malefic and aspected by a malefic, the entire year will be inauspicious. If the *Punya Saham* is aspected by a benefic planet—

---

-> If it is in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses from the Ascendant, it destroys virtue, fortune, and fame. If aspected by a benefic or its lord, happiness and virtue arise at the end. || 32 || <-
-> Joined with a malefic but aspected by a benefic, it is inauspicious at first and auspicious later. Joined with a benefic but aspected by a malefic, it is auspicious at first and bad later. || 33 || <-
-> In the year where the *Punya Saham* is auspicious, that year brings good; where it is unfavorable, it is not. Therefore, one should examine the *Punya Saham* first. || 34 || <-
-> If in the birth chart it was in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses, it acts like the twelfth house; if later suppressed by a malefic, then virtue, wealth, and happiness are destroyed. || 35 || <-

---

—then it is established by implication that the results will be auspicious for the entire duration of the year. || 33 || Now, he praises the importance of the *Punya Saham*. **In which year:** In whichever year the *Punya Saham* is auspicious (producing good results), that year is "auspicious-carrying." This means that year should be known as giving good results. In a year where the *Punya Saham* is unfavorable, it does not give good results during that year; it only gives inauspicious results. For this reason, one should examine the *Punya Saham* first and foremost. || 34 ||

Now, he describes the results according to the position of the *Punya Saham* in the birth chart. **In the birth:** In the birth chart <term>Sutau: Referring to the natal horoscope or 'Janma Kundali'</term>, if the Ascendant is in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses <note>original: "Rihphala" (twelfth house)</note>—

<vocab>Punya Saham (Lot of Virtue), Varshalagna (Year-Ascendant), Sastha-Ashta-Rihpha (6th, 8th, 12th houses), Subha-graha (Benefic planet), Papa-graha (Malefic planet), Jyotish (Astrology), Rihphala (12th house/Loss)</vocab>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Sahamas (Arabic Parts/Sensitive Points) in Tajika astrology, specifically focusing on the Punya Sahama (Point of Fortune) and the "malefic" Sahamas like death and disease.</meta>

If it is located in the twelfth house <note>The house of loss and expenditure</note>, this position becomes the Point of Fortune <note>original: "Punya-sahama"</note>. However, if it is further afflicted by malefic planets—either joined with them or aspected by them—it becomes a destroyer of wealth and happiness. Regarding the results: if the lord of the Point of Fortune is "burnt" <note>combust, or too close to the Sun</note> or set <note>heliacal setting</note>, the result is likewise the destruction of dharma, wealth, and happiness. || 35 ||

Regarding the Sahamas: All these Sahamas, including the Ascendant, should be contemplated by the astrologer in this manner at the time of birth. *Mandha* refers to disease; *Ari* refers to the enemy; *Kali* refers to a quarrel; *Mrityu* is well-known as death. Because these are indicators of inauspicious Sahamas, the Sahama of poverty is also included. Therefore, it will be stated later in another text: "For the points of poverty, death, disease, enmity, and quarrel, the rule is reversed." In these specific Sahamas, one should predict the result as the opposite of the result for the Point of Fortune. Furthermore, just like the Point of Fortune, if it reaches the seven positions of one's own desire...

---

-> Thus, one should contemplate all the Sahamas at the time of birth. For the points of disease, enmity, quarrel, and death, one should declare the result by reversal. || 36 || <-

-> The Sahama of Success <term>Karyasiddhi Sahama: A sensitive point calculated to determine the success of an undertaking</term>, when joined with or aspected by benefics and forming a <term>Muthashila: A specific aspect in Tajika astrology where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the fulfillment of an event</term> yoga, grants victory. If it is aspected or joined by both benefics and malefics, the wise say victory comes after a struggle. || 37 || <-

---

Starting from that verse, when performing an analysis of the fifty factors encountered at birth, if a "good" result is calculated for the Sahamas of disease, enmity, quarrel, or death, then an inauspicious result should be declared. If an "inappropriate" result is calculated, then a good result should be declared—this is the meaning of "reversal." || 36 ||

Now, he describes the auspicious results of the Sahama of Success. Regarding "Success": seeing the Sahama of Success joined with auspicious planets <note>benefics like Jupiter or Venus</note> or aspected by them. Likewise, if a *Muthashila* yoga is formed with auspicious planets, then it grants victory in battle, the destruction of enemies, and personal advancement. Immediately following that, if that Sahama of Success is seen or joined by both auspicious and inauspicious planets, then the wise (the scholars) declare that victory comes through distress or with a small amount of effort. "Battle" <note>original: "Sangara"</note> is an illustrative term that also includes lawsuits and disputes. || 37 ||

# 50

Now, the author describes the auspicious and inauspicious results of the **Lot of Strife** <note>original: *Kali-sahama*; 'Kali' refers to discord or conflict</note>. Regarding strife: if the Lot of Strife is associated with or aspected by planets that are mixed with malefic influences, it brings trouble. Specifically, if there is a conjunction with a malefic planet or a *Muthasila* <term>Muthasila: A Tajika astrological aspect where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the coming to fruition of an event</term> with a malefic, then the person faces death or a death-like situation due to quarrels <note>original: *jhakkata*, a vernacular term for a loud brawl or dispute</note>. Following this, if the Lot of Strife is joined with or aspected by benefics <note>original: *saumya*, literally 'gentle' or lunar planets like Jupiter or Venus</note>, the person achieves victory in the conflict and attains personal excellence. If the aspect is mixed (both benefic and malefic), then both victory and the pain of conflict occur; strife brings both a win and suffering.

### 38

Now, the author describes the auspicious and inauspicious results of the **Lot of Marriage** <note>original: *Vivaha-sahama*</note>. If the Lot of Marriage is joined with or aspected by its own lord <note>the planet ruling the sign where the lot falls</note>, or aspected by other benefic groups, it is favorable.

-> If the Lot of Strife is joined with mixed planets, it brings strife; if with malefic *Muthasila*, it brings death. If aspected by benefics, one gains victory; if mixed, one finds both strife and pain. <-

### 39

If the Lot of Marriage and its lord are aspected by or joined with benefics, or form a *Muthasila* with them, it is said to be auspicious. It brings peace and the successful performance of marriage. When the aspect is mixed with both good and bad planets, the marriage may proceed but with some complications. However, if the Lot of Marriage is joined with, aspected by, or forms a *Muthasila* with cruel malefic planets, or if it is connected to the Lord of the Eighth House <note>the house of death and endings</note>, then marriage will certainly not take place in that year.

### 39

If the Lord of Fame <note>original: *Yashodhipa*</note> is placed in the eighth house <note>the house of loss</note> and is joined with or aspected by a malefic, there is an immediate destruction of fame. If one is deprived of merit and gains ill-repute, their power is destroyed and the family's reputation is ruined.

### 40

If the Lord of the Lot of Fame is joined with or aspected by benefics, or forms a *Muthasila* with them, it brings auspicious peace. But if the Lord of Fame is in the eighth house <note>the house of hidden things and death</note> and is joined or aspected by a malefic planet...

The destruction of fame earned over a long period of time may occur. However, through association with malefic planets, there is a gain of infamy. ->**On Fame**<-

There is victory in battle and gain of vehicles and weapons. If there is an *Isarafa* yoga with a malefic, there is loss of wealth. || 41 ||

If the **Saham of Hope** and its lord are free from the sixth, eighth, and twelfth houses, and are associated with or aspected by gentle <note>benefic</note> planets, then one gains desired objects, excellent vehicles, and so on. If aspected by or joined with a cruel <note>malefic</note> planet, there is great sorrow. || 42 ||

If he forms an *Itthashala* yoga with the lord of death <note>the lord of the 8th house</note>, and is weak in strength, then death may occur. || 43 ||

If the Lord of the Lot of Sickness <note>original: "Roga Saham"</note> forms an *Ithashala* yoga <note>A specific planetary configuration in Tajika astrology indicating the coming together of influences</note> with the Lord of the Eighth House <note>The house associated with death and longevity</note>, then death or a fatal condition occurs. Now, the author describes the auspicious and inauspicious results of the Lot of Sickness <note>referred to here as "Mandha Saham"</note> once more. If the Lot of Sickness is aspected by its own Lord or by benefic planets <note>Saumya-graha; planets like Jupiter or Venus that bring positive influence</note>, or if it is occupied by them—this being an illustrative case—and if the Lord of the Lot is strong and not placed in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses <note>The "Dusthanas" or difficult houses in an astrological chart</note>, then sickness does not occur. However, if it is joined or aspected by both benefics and malefic planets, there is some fear of disease. || 44 ||

If the Lot of Wealth is aspected by or joined with its own Lord or benefic planets, it grants happiness through the arrival of wealth <note>Dravya</note>. If it is joined or aspected by malefic planets, it causes the destruction and loss of wealth.

-> If the Lot is aspected by malefics but forms an *Ithashala* yoga with a benefic planet, it first destroys the previous wealth, but later causes the arising of wealth accompanied by happiness. If the situation is reversed— <-

If the Lot of Wealth is joined or aspected by malefic planets, it causes the loss and expenditure of money. However, if that same Lot, while being influenced by malefics, is also aspected by benefics or forms an *Ithashala* yoga with a benefic planet, then all the previously thought-of wealth is destroyed first, but subsequently, the production of wealth occurs with happiness. "By reversal" <note>original: "vyatyasato"</note> means: if the Lot of Wealth is aspected by benefics and forms an *Ithashala* yoga with them, then the result is entirely auspicious and no destruction of wealth occurs. || 45 ||

Now he describes the results of aspects from enemies and friends. **Regarding the aspect of an enemy:** Whichever *Sahma* <note>A calculated sensitive point in a horoscope, similar to the Arabic Parts</note> is viewed by a planet—whether naturally auspicious or inauspicious—via an "enemy aspect" <note>original: "ripu-drishti"; in Tajika astrology, certain angular relationships are considered hostile</note>, there will be fear arising from enemies and a loss of wealth due to thieves. Now, the author describes the auspicious and inauspicious results of the **Father's Lot** <note>original: *Pitr-sahama*; a specific mathematical point in the horoscope representing the father</note>. Regarding the Father's Lot: if the Father's Lot is aspected <note>viewed by</note> or conjoined with benefic planets, or if it is aspected or conjoined by its own planetary lord, it bestows wealth, fine clothing, honor, and mental happiness upon the father. However, if the lord of the Father's Lot is devoid of strength, or is combust <note>too close to the sun</note>, or is situated in the eighth house <note>the house of death</note> from the Ascendant, and if there is a *Musarafa* yoga <note>a specific planetary configuration in Tajika astrology indicating the separation or waning of influence</note> with a malefic planet—especially if it occurs in a moveable sign like Capricorn—then the father may face death during a journey to a foreign land. || 49 ||

Next, he further explains the mixed results of the Father's Lot. If there is a benefic influence: because of the general rule, this applies to the **Son's Lot** as well. If there is an *Ithasala* <note>original: *it-thasala*; a planetary application or connection</note> with a malefic planet, there will first be a great provocation of disease in the first half of the year.

> If the Father's Lot is aspected or joined by its lord, the father gains wealth, clothing, honor, and happiness. If the lord is weak, in the eighth house, or in a *Musarafa* with a malefic, the father perishes while traveling abroad. || 49 || If there is an *Ithasala* with a malefic, there is first a great outbreak of illness. Afterward, happiness is found; if the lord possesses full strength, there is an increase in honor and fame from the king. || 50 || If the **Imprisonment Lot** <note>original: *Bandhana-sahama*</note> is joined or aspected by its lord, there is no imprisonment. If it is joined or aspected by a malefic, imprisonment occurs, especially if the lord is in a malefic *Ithasala*. || 51 ||

The provocation of disease and its increase will be great at first. Afterward, in the second half of the year, one finds happiness; this means one becomes free from disease. If the "Lord" (the master of the Father's Lot) possesses "Full Strength" <note>original: *purna-virya*; specifically 15 to 20 units of strength</note>, there will be an increase in honor and fame from the presence of the King. || 50 ||

Now he describes the auspicious and inauspicious results of the **Imprisonment Lot**. Regarding imprisonment: if the Imprisonment Lot is conjoined with or aspected by its own lord, then there is no imprisonment or confinement in jail. If that Imprisonment Lot is aspected or conjoined by a malefic planet, then there is imprisonment. Furthermore, if the lord of that lot is in a conjunction or aspect with a malefic planet, then imprisonment is especially certain. || 51 ||

## Now, the auspicious and inauspicious results of the Part of Honor are described:

**Regarding the "Gaurava" <note>The Part of Honor or Dignity</note>:** If the Part of Honor is joined with or aspected by its own lord or by <term>benefic planets</term> <note>planets like Jupiter or Venus that bring positive influence</note>, then it leads to the attainment of happiness. One obtains royal honor, fame, and fine garments. If the Part of Honor is joined with or aspected by <term>malefic planets</term> <note>planets like Mars or Saturn that bring challenging influence</note>, then there will be a loss of position and authority. || 51 ||

Now, further auspicious results of the Part of Honor are described: **Regarding the Benefics and Malefics:** If the Part of Honor is aspected by or joined with both benefic and malefic planets, or if it forms a <term>Muthashila</term> <note>original: "मुथशिल" - a specific aspect in Tajika astrology where a faster planet approaches a slower one, indicating the fulfillment of an event</note> with malefic planets, then in the first half of the year, it causes loss of wealth and loss of prestige. However, in the latter half of the year, if it forms a <term>Muthashila</term> with benefic planets—

-> If the Part of Honor is joined with the lord of the earth <note>the ruling planet</note> and benefics, it leads to happiness. If joined with malefics, there is loss of land and position. <-

—then it bestows happiness, vehicles, and the acquisition of weapons <note>historically, a sign of status or military success</note>. || 52-53 ||

**The Part of Action and the Tenth House:** If the lords of the <term>Karma Bhava</term> <note>the 10th house of profession</note> and the <term>Karma Sahama</term> <note>the Part of Action</note> are endowed with strength and form a <term>Muthashila</term> with benefic planets or their own lords, they grant the acquisition of gold, horses, elephants, and land. If they are aspected by or joined with malefic planets, they grant inauspicious results. || 54 ||

If these lords are <term>combust</term> <note>original: "दग्धा" (burnt) - too close to the Sun to be effective</note> or <term>retrograde</term> <note>original: "वक्रा" - appearing to move backward</note>, they cause the failure of actions. If they are joined with or aspected by <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: "सौरि" (son of the Sun)</note>, then specifically, there is a fall from power, destruction of work, and failure in royal service. || 55 ||

# Page 53
## Chapter 1

...if auspicious works are undertaken, they should be desired according to the ritual timing. If the lords of the House of Action <note>Karma-bhava: the 10th house of career and social status</note> and the Sahama <note>term: Sahama (Arabic: Sahm). A calculated sensitive point in a horoscope used for specific predictions.</note> are joined with or aspected by Saturn, then specifically there will be a failure in one's actions. Likewise, if the lords of the King’s Work and the lords of the Sahama of Royalty are in a <term>Musaripha</term> <note>original: "Mūsarīpha". An aspect in Tajika astrology where a faster planet separates from a slower one, often indicating a loss or fading influence.</note> yoga with a strong malefic planet, then there will be a fall from power, destruction of the kingdom, loss of profession, and the ruin of any auspicious work already commenced. || 55 ||

Now, the author explains the path for clarifying ambiguous terms regarding the Sahamas. Regarding the word "Teacher":

The one who instructs in virtues, such as the Gayatri mantra and so on, is called the "Teacher" here. Although the *Amarakosha* <note>A famous Sanskrit lexicon</note> defines "Guru" as both Jupiter and elders like the father, here—because it is mentioned in conjunction with the Sahama of the Father and malefic influences—it refers to the specific instructor. The attainment of results from Jupiter would be impossible in this specific context; therefore, it refers to the Teacher's instruction. Similarly, by the word "Knowledge" <note>Vidya</note>, wisdom is meant. By the word "Scripture" <note>Shastra</note>, the revealed texts <note>Shruti</note> and remembered traditions <note>Smriti</note> are meant. By the word "Dullness" <note>Jadya</note>, delusion or ignorance is meant.

---

-> The Teacher, the Guru, Knowledge, Learning, Scripture, Revelation, and Tradition; <-
-> Delusion, Dullness, Strength, the Army, the Limbs of the Body, Water, and Radiance. || 56 ||
-> Heaviness (Dignity), Lordship over a District, Gravity, Being possessed of Honor; <-
-> Restraint, Favor, Prosperity, and the King who bears the signs of the Umbrella and so on. || 57 ||

---

The word "Strength" <note>Bala</note> is well-known to mean both the army and physical power. The author clarifies the meaning: by "Strength," the army is meant; by "Body," the limbs or the physical frame is meant. By "Water," the radiance or luster of the complexion is meant. || 56 ||

Now, regarding the word "Guru" (Heavy/Great), from which the terms "Heaviness" <note>Gurutā</note> and "Gravity" <note>Gaurava</note> arise: one might doubt if these should be stated separately since their meanings are so similar. To address this distinction, he says: "Heaviness." Here, common usage is the guide. By "Heaviness," he means "Lordship over a District" or sovereignty over a region. By "Gravity," he means "Being possessed of Honor" or an abundance of prestige. 

Now, the word "King" <note>Raja</note> is famously a synonym for the Warrior caste <note>Kshatriya</note>. He clarifies the meaning through "Restraint" and so on. In common worldly usage, the word "King" refers only to the Lord of the Earth. He defines his characteristics: "Restraint" refers to the power to inflict punishment or cause undesirable outcomes for others...

<summary>This page defines technical astrological terms used in Tajika astrology, distinguishing between literal meanings and specific divinatory applications for career, royalty, and personal qualities. It explains how malefic planetary aspects like the Musaripha yoga can lead to a loss of status or power.</summary>

<keywords>Tajika astrology, Sahama, Musaripha yoga, 10th House, Guru, Shastra, Kingship, Sanskrit terminology, planetary aspects</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the lexicographical or technical analysis of terms related to royal attributes, human conditions, and social roles, likely from a commentary on a Nighantu (glossary) or similar shastra.</meta>

Favor <note>original: "Anugraha"</note> is defined as the creation of speech; the Lord <note>Vibhu</note> is capable of this. What is his nature? He possesses signs such as the parasol. The parasol is well-known. By the word "etcetera," signs such as the fly-whisk are included; he who possesses or serves these signs is "possessing signs" <note>lingabhak</note>. || 57 || Now, he explains the meanings of majesty, capability, and valor. || Majesty || The state of being great is majesty, or greatness. The depth of counsel: "counsel" <note>mantra</note> refers to deliberations made in private, and its "depth" is its non-disclosure. If one asks how it remains undisclosed, he says: "Resolution." Resolution is courage. "Intellect" is wisdom born of mature reflection. One who is endowed with these is "possessing" them; this is the application. This inherent quality is the nature of majesty. By the word "capability" <note>samarthya</note>, physical strength is meant. By the word "valor" <note>shaurya</note>, the effort involved in subduing enemies is meant. || 58 || Hope, etc. || By the word "hope" <note>asha</note>, desire is expressed. By the word "faith" <note>shraddha</note>, a righteous mind is meant. "Vanda" <insert>salutation/dependence</insert>—

-> Majesty is the possession of deep counsel, resolution, intellect, and the like. Capability is physical strength. Valor is the effort in subduing enemies. || 58 || <-
-> Hope is called desire. Faith is a righteous mind. Dependence is relying on others. The falling of water is rain, or the immersion of people in water. || 59 || <-
-> Mental anguish and physical disease are known as "afflictions" arising from the beginning and end of the physical body. The merchant's trade is a mixture of truth and falsehood. Birth is known as "conception." || 60 || <-
-> Service is performing the work of others; other terms are clear from their own names. These should be examined according to lineage, caste, and inherent nature. || 61 || <-

—by the word "Vanda," dependence or being under the control of another is meant. By the phrase "falling of water," rain is meant, or alternatively, the sudden immersion in water. || 59 || Mental anguish, etc. || By the word "heat" <note>tapa</note>, mental anguish <note>adhi</note> is meant. Disease <note>vyadhi</note> is that which causes pain to the body. By the word "kinsmen," blood relatives <note>sapinda</note> are spoken of. By the word "merchant," the "truth-falsehood" profession is meant—living by a mixture of truth and lies. By the word "birth," conception is meant. || 60 || Service, etc. || By the phrase "work for others," service <note>dasatva</note> is expressed. The others are clear from their own names. They should be defined appropriately according to lineage, caste, and nature. || 62 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion on astrological Sahamas (sensitive points) and transitions into the calculation of planetary periods (Dashas) for the year.</meta>

Page 54
Chapter 1

Regarding the conjunction with auspicious planets... When a <term>Sahama</term><note>A Sahama is a specific sensitive point in a horoscope calculated by adding and subtracting the positions of planets and house cusps, similar to Arabic Parts.</note> is joined with or aspected by an auspicious planet, the happiness resulting from that Sahama should be understood according to the strength of the Sahama's lord. For example: when the lord of the Sahama is very strong, there is great happiness. When it has medium strength, the happiness is moderate. When it is weak, there is a lack of happiness or gain. Poverty, extreme sluggishness, and so on are said to occur in the opposite case. This result should be understood as the reverse for inauspicious Sahamas. For example: when a Sahama indicating illness or strife is reached, it should be known as inauspicious. When an inauspicious result has arrived, it is known as unfavorable. || 62 ||

Now, he explains by what means the consideration of auspicious and inauspicious Sahamas in the fifth house of the birth chart should occur. "At the time of the query..." The phrase "Sahama at the time of the query" uses the singular to represent the class, meaning "all Sahamas." These Sahamas, such as the <term>Punya Sahama</term><note>The Sahama of Merit or Fortune.</note>, should be considered according to the desire of the querent (the person asking the question).

-> Happiness comes from the aspect or conjunction of auspicious planets, according to their strength. Poverty, extreme sluggishness, and so on are described as the opposite. || 62 || <-
-> Even at the time of a query, the Sahama should be considered according to the querent's wish. All of them are useful here, as people ask about various wonderful things. || 63 || <-

Thus ends the consideration of the results of the Sahamas.

-> Having set aside the signs of the clarified Sahamas, first place the one with the fewest degrees. Then, place those with more degrees in sequence; this shall be the order of the <term>Sudasha</term><note>A specific system of planetary periods or 'timing' used in annual astrology.</note> here. || 1 || <-

Thus ends the consideration of the results of the Sahamas. Now he describes the bringing forth of the <term>Dasha</term><note>A period of time ruled by a specific planet or point that determines when certain events will manifest.</note>. First, he explains the means of achieving the Dasha. "The clarified ones..." At the time of the <term>Varshapravesha</term><note>The solar return, or the exact moment the Sun returns to its birth position, marking the start of the new astrological year.</note>, one should calculate the clarified Sahamas, discard the zodiac signs, and set down all the degrees and minutes. Among them, the Sahama with the <term>Alpalava</term><note>The smallest degree.</note> (the lowest degree) should be placed first. After that, one should arrange them in order of increasing degrees. For example: just as the degrees of the Sahamas increase from the lowest, so too shall be the sequence of the Dashas corresponding to those Sahamas. This shall be the sequence of the Dashas for the year. || 2 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of planetary period calculations, specifically focusing on the mathematical derivation of the "constant" used to determine the length of a planetary influence (Dasha).</meta>

->### Now, the method for calculating the Patyansha (the degrees of planetary portion) is described.<-

**Regarding the subtraction:** The planet with the smaller degree should be subtracted from the one with the higher degree in a sequential order. For example: the first (lowest) degree is subtracted from the second, the second from the third, and so on, following the order of the planets. After this, the sum of these "purified degrees" <note>original: *shuddhamshaka*; these are the incremental differences between planetary positions</note> is calculated. Through this mutual subtraction, the total sum of the differences will always equal the highest degree among all the planets. This is a universal rule. 

Then, one must divide the length of the year—either the solar year of 360 days or the civil year <note>original: *savana*; a year of approximately 365 days, 15 minutes, 31 seconds, and 30 thirds</note>—by this total sum of differences. The resulting quotient is known as the **Constant Number** <note>term: *Dhruvanka* - a fixed multiplier used for further calculations</note>. || 2 ||

### Now, the method for bringing forth the days is described:

**Regarding the purified degrees:** All the purified degrees should be multiplied by this obtained Constant Number. By doing this, the measure of the period <note>term: *Dasha* - a specific time window where a planet's influence is dominant</note> in days and so on is determined for each respective planet. 

->The smaller is subtracted from the larger in sequence; the sum of the purified degree remainders<-
->is exactly equal to the highest degree of all. By dividing the year by this,<-
->the calculation is well-performed. || 2 ||<-
->Multiply those purified degrees by this obtained Constant Number; the result is the measure<-
->of the period in days for that planet. One should then declare the results according to the scriptures. || 3 ||<-
->If the purified degrees are equal, the period belongs to the stronger planet. If the strength is equal,<-
->the one with the slower motion comes first. If those are equal, the calculation involves the Ascendant,<-
->considering the strength and weakness of the celestial bodies. || 4 ||<-

After calculating the days, one should announce the auspicious or inauspicious results from the traditional texts. || 3 ||

### Now, the decision on the period (Dasha) when degrees are equal:

**Regarding the equality of purified degrees:** If two planets have the same degree, the first period belongs to the one who is stronger in the five-fold strength test <note>original: *panchavarga*; a traditional Vedic method for assessing planetary power</note>. 

<margin>Note: Some manuscripts read "equality of purified degrees," but the reading "equality of lesser degrees" is more logical and accepted by all authorities.</margin>

The order of the periods follows the sequence of the degrees, starting from the lowest. Because the rule states that purified degrees arise only after the subtraction is performed, if two planets have the same purified degree, the one with greater strength takes the first period, followed by the second. If their strength is also equal, the text says: **"Regarding strength..."**—if the strength is equal, the planet with the slower motion <note>original: *nyuna-gateh*; referring to the daily velocity of the planet</note> takes precedence.

<summary>This page outlines the mathematical procedure for calculating the "Dhruvanka" (constant) and the specific duration of planetary periods (Dashas) by subtracting planetary degrees in sequence. It also provides tie-breaking rules for determining the order of influence based on planetary strength and speed when degrees are equal.</summary>

<keywords>Patyansha, Dhruvanka, Dasha calculation, purified degrees, planetary strength, Panchavarga, solar year, civil year, mathematical astrology</keywords>

--- Page 108 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of calculating planetary periods (Dashas) within a solar return chart (Varsha-pravesha). It focuses on the mathematical conversion of zodiacal positions into time units.</meta>

In the place of signs (<term>Rashi</term> <note>A zodiac sign of 30 degrees</note>), days should be added; in the place of minutes (<term>Kala</term>), ghatikas <note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> should be added; and in the place of seconds (<term>Vikala</term>), palas <note>A unit of time equal to 24 seconds</note> should be applied. Seconds divided by sixty: the quotient should be added to the minutes.

Minutes divided by sixty: the quotient should be added to the degrees (<term>Amsha</term>). Degrees divided by thirty: the quotient should be added to the signs. Signs divided by twelve: the remaining degrees, along with the minutes, are thus calculated by twelve.

The remainder represents the signs. When the Sun reaches such a position, it marks the entry into the second period (<term>Dasha</term>). By adding the days to the Sun's position at the start of the year, the entry into the second period is found, and so on for the following periods. By this method of addition, if the final Sun position equals the Sun's position at the time of the year's entry (<term>Varsha-pravesha</term>), then this <term>Solar Dasha Sequence</term> <note>original: "Saura-dasha-krama"</note> is correct; otherwise, it is incorrect.

Now, regarding the <term>Civil Dasha</term> <note>original: "Savana-dasha," based on a 360-day year</note>: At the moment of the year's entry, the first Civil Dasha begins. Then, from the weekday of the year's entry, the days of the first Civil Dasha are added; in that month, on such a weekday, the second Dasha begins. Then, from the weekday of the second Dasha's entry, the days of the second Dasha are added; in that month, on such a weekday, the third Dasha begins, and so on. If, after adding all periods, the weekday of the next year's entry is reached, then the Civil Dasha entry is correct; otherwise, it is incorrect.

**Example**

Saturn (<term>Shani</term>) has the lowest degrees of all; therefore, leaving aside the sign, Saturn is placed first: 6|48|54. Then the Sun (<term>Arka</term>): 7|26|16. Then Mercury (<term>Budha</term>): 12|11|18. Then Mars (<term>Bhauma</term>): 14|32|36. Then the Ascendant (<term>Lagna</term>): 18|35|54. Then the Moon (<term>Chandra</term>): 22|35|47. Then Venus (<term>Shukra</term>): 24|31|48. Then Jupiter (<term>Guru</term>): 29|35|53.

First, the degrees to be protected for Saturn are as they stand: 6|48|54. These are subtracted from the Sun's degrees 7|20|6 <note>The text likely refers to a specific calculation of intervals between planets</note>, resulting in the Sun's degrees to be protected: 0|44|12. In this way, the previous values are subtracted from the subsequent ones to find the "degrees to be protected" (<term>Palyamsha</term>) for all.

Mercury: 4|45|2; Sign: 6|22; Ascendant: 3|12|18; Moon: 4|55|3; Venus: 2|22|1; Jupiter: 5|32|25. The sum of these is 29|35|53. With this, the year measure of 360 days, converted to seconds, is 1,296,000. The divisor is 106,513, resulting in a multiplier (<term>Gunaka</term>) of 12|10|3. By this, Saturn's degrees 6|48|54 are multiplied, resulting in Saturn's Dasha days: 82|55|18.

Alternatively, using the sum of degrees 29|35|53 and the solar year measure of 365|15|31, the multiplier becomes 12|20|42. By this, for Saturn...

<summary>This page provides mathematical tables for calculating planetary periods (dashas) using two different time-keeping systems: the 360-day Savana year and the Solar year. It includes specific breakdowns for Saturn's sub-periods and instructions on how to align these periods with the Sun's position at the start of the year.</summary>

--- Page 110 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating planetary periods (dashas) within a solar return or yearly horoscope. The text transitions from solar-based calculations to the Savana (360-day) year system.</meta>

<unclear>Sha</unclear> positions should be added to the days; <term>minutes of arc</term><note>original: kala</note> positions should be added to the ghatikas <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>; <term>seconds of arc</term><note>original: vikala</note> positions should be added to the palas <note>a unit of time equal to 24 seconds</note>. The <term>seconds of arc</term> divided by five: the quotient should be added to the minutes.

The <term>minutes of arc</term> divided by five: the quotient should be added to the degrees. The <term>degrees</term><note>original: amsha</note> divided by thirty: the quotient should be added to the signs. If the <term>signs</term><note>original: rashi</note> exceed twelve, they are to be divided by twelve and the remainder taken. To the position of the Sun at the time of the solar return, add these calculated days to find the Sun's position at the entry of the second <term>planetary period</term><note>original: dasha; a period of time ruled by a specific planet</note>. The entry of the second <term>period</term> occurs when the Sun reaches that position; the same applies to the subsequent ones. In this way, by addition, the final Sun position becomes equal to the Sun's position at the start of the next year's return. If so, the <term>solar period sequence</term><note>original: saura-dashakrama</note> is correct; otherwise, it is incorrect.

Now, regarding the Savana period: The first Savana <term>period</term> begins at the moment of the year's commencement. Then, the days of the first Savana <term>period</term> are added to the weekday and time of the year's commencement; the second <term>period</term> begins on that resulting weekday and time at the end of that month-segment. Similarly, the days of the second <term>period</term> are added to the weekday and time of the second <term>period's</term> entry; the third <term>period</term> begins on that resulting weekday and time. The same follows for the rest. When added thus, if the weekday and time of the next year's commencement are reached, then the <term>period</term> entry is correct—otherwise, it is incorrect.

**Example:** <term>Saturn</term><note>original: Shani</note> has the fewest degrees relative to all others. For this reason, leaving aside the night, Saturn is placed first at 6;48;54. Then the Sun <note>original: Arka/Ravi</note> at 7;2;16. Then <term>Mercury</term><note>original: Budha</note> at 12;12;58. Then <term>Mars</term><note>original: Bhauma</note> at 14;22;36. Then the <term>Ascendant</term><note>original: Lagna; the rising sign</note> at 18;3;54. Then the Moon <note>original: Chandra</note> at 22;17;47. Then <term>Venus</term><note>original: Shukra</note> at 24;31;48. Then <term>Jupiter</term><note>original: Guru</note> at 29;35;32.

First, the degrees to be subtracted for Saturn are 6;48;54 as they stand. These are subtracted from the Sun's 7;3;16 <note>Note: there is a slight discrepancy in the digits here in the source</note> to get the Sun's subtractive degrees: 0;14;22. In this manner, the preceding values are subtracted from the subsequent ones to find all the subtractive degrees: <term>Mercury</term>: 4;46;12; <term>Mars</term>: 2;10;38; <term>Ascendant</term>: 3;41;18; Moon: 4;15;3; <term>Venus</term>: 2;14;1; <term>Jupiter</term>: 5;3;35. The sum of these is 29;35;32. With this, the year measure of 360 is used. 1,296,000 <note>total seconds in a circle</note> is the dividend, and 106,593 is the divisor, resulting in a multiplier of 12;10;3. By this, Saturn's degrees of 6;48;54 are multiplied, resulting in Saturn's period of 82 days, 55 ghatikas, and 18 palas. Alternatively, the year measure of 360 is divided by the sum of the degrees (29;35;32), giving a multiplier of 12;10;42. By this, for Saturn...

It details how to derive specific time intervals from the longitudinal distances between planets.</summary>
